# Burro website: design

Status: design for the first build, 2026-09-23. The foundation of `apps/web` is built: the API types and client, the recorded answers, the tokens, the shell, and the methods, sources and accessibility pages. So is the search page, with its chips, its settings, its result cards and its map. So are the page of an area, the comparison, sharing, the sitemap and the robots file. The parts were then put together and gone over as one website: the answers were recorded again from the API as it then stood (engine 1.3.0; it moved three times while this was done, from 1.1.0, and the recordings followed it each time), every built page was read, one whole visit was walked against the service's own answers, and the website was answered with what it did not expect. What each of these settled is written in where it applies. It has since been run in a browser once, and read by two reviewers. The browser showed four things no test could: the map drew nothing, a button lay over the map, there was no icon, and a strength was given as a trade-off. The reviewers found nineteen more. What was put right is written in where it applies, each with the test that would have caught it. It was then walked through in a browser against the service itself, at the width of a phone and of a desk, by mouse and by keyboard. That found nineteen things more, of which the gravest was that a sentence could lose its budget and its workplace and the page ranked as if it had read them. What the website could put right is written in where it applies, and what needs the service is in section 13, gaps 17 to 22. Section 9 ends with the checks that must be made by hand, because no test can make them. The README says what to type to see the website in a browser. The search page was then rebuilt to [slice 1](slice-1.md), against contract version 2 and engine 1.4.0: the answer comes first, a vibe is shown as a band between two ends, a search can start from a shelf of words, and what the reader noticed and did not apply is offered and never guessed. Sections 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 say what that changed. Section 4.1 says what a results page holds before anything is opened, and what was measured of it in a browser. Section 13 says which gaps the contract has closed. The page of an area was then rebuilt to open with the portrait, and the comparison, the areas most like an area and a page of vibes were built with it. Sections 2, 4.2, 5.3, 7, 9, 10 and 12 say what that changed, and section 4.2 what was measured of it in a browser. The census figures of an area were then built on the made-up city, as the last of the parts of its page that are closed: section 2.1 gives the rules of how they are shown, and what was measured of them in a browser at both sizes. The box was then mended, on 2026-09-26, so that what is added to a search is what is sent, and what a person set by hand is not set back by words the search has already read. Sections 1, 3, 5.3, 7, 9 and 10 say what that changed. The mended box was then walked in a browser, press by press, to lose a value that was set by hand. Two ways were found: words that were read, taken out of the box and put back by the keys that undo, were read a second time, and so was a copy of them that was pasted into the box. Sections 1, 3, 5.3, 7, 9 and 10 say what that settled. What settled it was then tried on many boxes changed at random, with every word followed, and walked again. That found a third way, which was seen in a browser: a sentence that was copied before it was sent, and pasted once the box had been typed over, was read again. It found a fourth that was not seen in one, where the keys that undo are pressed until they lead further back than the search knew. And it found words that were never read, and were not sent, with nothing said of it. Section 3 says what that settled, and what is still not known. The website was then given a look, on 2026-09-26: Town Map, the map of a gentle game ([ADR 0033](../adr/0033-the-website-looks-like-the-map-of-a-gentle-game.md)). What a press does, what is sent and what is said stayed as they were. Four things changed about where things stand: the first screen is one box with three helpers under it, from 80rem the settings stand open beside the answer, a slider is drawn as a gauge of steps, and while a search is read a rabbit hops where the results will stand ([ADR 0035](../adr/0035-the-search-page-is-one-box-and-the-look-gives-way-to-the-answer.md)). Every page was dressed, and walked in a browser at both sizes. Sections 1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 11 say what that changed, sections 4.1 and 4.2 what was measured of it, and [the design of the look](look.md) where each part of the look is changed, how it is turned down, and what is still open. The founder then walked through the website, that afternoon, and it was rebuilt to what they wrote. A search begins at one of two tabs, "Quick search" and "Deep search", with the map in sight on both. After a search the page is the answer and a larger map, and the settings are one fold, "Refine search". The settings are one accordion. No edge is dashed, the chip of the usual settings is gone, and nothing beside what Burro understood says who read the words. Comparing areas is a headline ([ADR 0039](../adr/0039-comparing-areas-is-a-headline-of-the-website-and-nobody-wins-a-comparison.md)). The pages that explain lead with a key and fold their working ([ADR 0040](../adr/0040-a-page-that-explains-leads-with-a-key-and-folds-its-working.md)). The rabbit always moves, and can be stopped. And the website's own words were written again, for a person who has never seen Burro ([ADR 0038](../adr/0038-the-words-of-the-website-are-written-for-a-person-who-has-never-seen-burro.md), and [the guide to the words](words.md)). Sections 1 to 9 say what that changed. The website was then walked in a browser and mended, that afternoon: the first result of a desk came back into sight, Deep search came to make a search by its button alone, the order of the page came to be the order it is drawn in, every fold came to be one bar, and the rabbit came onto every page. **The founder walked it a second time that evening, and it was rebuilt again to what they wrote.** The page asks nothing: it applies what it read of a sentence, and each thing is a chip that can be taken off ([ADR 0012](../adr/0012-a-closed-vocabulary-and-a-guard-on-the-model.md), as amended). A search may be a visit ([ADR 0041](../adr/0041-a-search-may-be-a-visit.md)). The box is headed in the founder's own words, and nothing beside it says how words are handled. The settings are named "Space requirements", a switch says in a word whether it is on, and nothing moves as one is pressed. The rabbit has no button, and a first search waits with him. A result has no line on little towns, and its gauges stand in one column. A comparison leads back to the search. No page tells a visitor that a vibe is a rough guide. And the statement of accessibility is gone, with what the website said of a standard ([ADR 0042](../adr/0042-the-website-makes-no-claim-of-a-standard-of-accessibility.md)). Sections 1 to 10 say what that changed, each where it applies, and what stood before is kept where it says why a thing is as it is. **The website as it stands since was measured in a browser on 2026-09-26, at 390 by 844 and at 1440 by 900**: sections 4.1 and 4.2 give what was measured, each before what was measured earlier, and section 9 says which of its checks were made. **The founder walked it a third time, late that night, and on 2026-09-27 it was rebuilt to what they wrote.** A result shows each thing that was asked for as a name and a gauge, with a picture at each end of the gauge, and says "approx data" where a figure is not whole. It says why an area fits in no sentence, and the key of every source stands under its working ([ADR 0046](../adr/0046-a-result-shows-what-was-asked-for-as-a-name-and-a-gauge-and-the-source-of-every-figure-stands-under-its-working.md)). Every gauge of the website has a picture at each end. What an area gives up is drawn with a pair of scales, on a band of its own. Three groups of the settings have a drawing where they had a plain box. The rabbit hops more slowly, a first search waits longer with him, and soil flicks up from his hole. Sections 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 8 and 9 say what that changed, and section 4.1 what was measured of it. Where this document quotes the website's own words, it quotes them as they stood when the sentence was written: `apps/web/src/content/` has each as it is. So with the words of the service, which were written again on 2026-09-26 for a person who has never seen Burro: `packages/core` has each as it is, and the recorded answers show them.

This is the single source for the website in `apps/web`. It applies [PLAN](../PLAN.md) sections 4, 5, 8 and 9, ADRs [0002](../adr/0002-deterministic-core.md), [0005](../adr/0005-raw-prompts-are-never-stored.md), [0006](../adr/0006-rank-places-not-residents.md), [0008](../adr/0008-package-sources.md) and [0011](../adr/0011-nothing-is-kept-for-a-search.md), and [the contract](contract.md) sections 4, 5, 8 and 9. Where this document and the contract disagree, the contract is right and this document changes. Three people build three parts: section 11 says who owns what and where the parts meet. Section 13 lists what the contract does not yet give the website, and what the website does until it does.

## 0. Terms and the three rules

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Route N | The route with that number in contract section 9.2 |
| A type in `code` | The schema of that name in `contracts/openapi.json`, generated into `src/lib/api/schema.d.ts`. Never written by hand |
| API text | A string the API returned: a sentence's `text`, `notice_text`, an error's `message`, a fact's `label`, or a value in its `slots` |
| Site copy | Words kept in `src/content/`. They name controls, states and codes. They never hold a number or a proper noun about a place |
| The store | The one object in memory that holds a search (section 5.3) |

1. **Words about a place are API text.** The website lays API text out. It does not write, join or reword it. It formats no number that a fact's `slots` hold already formatted. It never prints a percentile: `slots.standing` says where a figure sits, and is literally true.
2. **The spec on screen is the last spec the API returned.** The website builds edits, never specs.
3. **What a person types stays in the box it was typed in, and in one `POST` body.** Section 10 lists everything else that is held, and where.

## 1. The first thirty seconds

The person has never been to London and arrives at `/` from a link. "May take" is a budget on broadband to a service in London. None has been measured against a service in London.

| # | Clock | The person | On screen | Calls | May take |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0 s | Arrives | The banner that says the data is made up, and the name board, which holds the name of the website and three links. The heading, "Find your area", with Burro the rabbit sitting beside it: he moves as a rabbit at rest does, and nothing on the page stops him. Under the heading, in a sentence or two, who Burro is for. Then two tabs, "Quick search", which is chosen, and "Deep search". The panel of Quick search holds one labelled box, "Tell me what's important in your space", with Search; a few sentences that say what a useful sentence holds, and one whole sentence as an example; and two helpers, each closed until it is pressed: "Try an example" and "Start from a word". Nothing beside the box says how words are handled. The map of every area in one plain colour: beside the tabs on a wide screen, and on a screen of one column over them, as a low strip with "Show the whole map" over it. The table of all areas is one press from the map, and before a search on one column it comes with the whole map | Route 5, from the browser | Shell in 1 s, map in 2.5 s. No layout shift: every block has its size before it has its content |
| 2 | 2 to 15 s | Types a sentence. May open a helper, choose the other tab, and pick a place | What they type. A count of characters left, once fewer than 100 remain of `limits.max_text`. What a helper opens stands under the line of helpers, or, since 2026-09-27, over it where a hand opened it and the whole of it has room there and none under, so that the helper stays where it was pressed. One is open at a time: three example sentences that fill the box and send nothing; and the shelf of seven everyday words, each a vibe, with the way to the rest, where a word opens the card of its vibe in place and colours the map by it, and sends nothing. "Deep search" shows its panel in the place of the other: what it is, in one sentence that is said once; then the settings, under their name, "Space requirements", as one accordion with its first two groups open, of which the first asks whether a person is renting, buying or visiting, and the second holds the field that finds a place to reach; then the button that makes a search of them, "Search with these requirements"; and at its foot a field that finds an area by its name. What is chosen there is kept, and nothing is ranked until the button is pressed. The map stays where it is, and as large as it is, as the tab changes. Changing tab loses nothing: what was typed is in the box when a person comes back to it, and a setting that was moved stays moved. Place options under the search field | Route 8, after 250 ms with no key pressed and 2 characters or more | Options in 300 ms |
| 3 | 15 s | Presses Enter or "Search" | At once: the button reads "Reading", and "Stop" stands beside it. Where the results will stand, Burro hops in and out of his hole, in a box that keeps the room of the answer, with "Reading your search" under him. He hops there too while a first ranking that a control asked for is worked out, with "Working out the ranking" under him. As he comes up and as he goes down, soil flicks up from his hole. A search that begins with no result in sight is shown no sooner than he has gone down his hole and come up three times, however soon the answer is in: the founder asked that he is seen, and then that he is seen for longer. Where the system asks for less movement he sits beside his hole, still, and nothing is waited for. The live region says "Reading your search" | Route 1, with `text` and the store's spec if it has one | 0.1 s to acknowledge, 1.2 s typical. The API gives a model 6 s and then answers by rules. The website waits 8 s, then shows the form (section 3). A first answer is shown after 3.75 s at the least, which is the wait: it was 2 s until 2026-09-27 |
| 4 | 16 s | Reads what Burro understood | Directly under the box, so that it is on screen when Search is pressed: what happened, in a line; then, in one line, what was left out of the search, which names each thing Burro noticed and did not take, and that some of the words were not read, and opens to why; any notice or failure; then the chips, which wrap and are never cut: what was asked for by way of character first, six at most. A chip says every part of itself, and what Burro took without being told it in so many words says "assumed". Nothing is asked, and nothing is offered to be pressed. After what was understood, and before the results, the settings: one fold named "Refine search", closed. On a narrow screen it is drawn directly after the first result, so that the first result is whole on the first screen, and comes after it in the page too. The box is one line high, and shows that line whole: while nobody types in it, what does not fit on the line is out of sight. While it is typed in, and while words are selected in it, the box wraps and is three lines high. It gives that room back when the sentence is sent, when it is left by keyboard, and once a press elsewhere has landed, and never under the press: what stands under the box would move between the button going down and coming up, and the press would land on nothing. The two tabs go, with whatever a helper held open, and "Start again" stands beside Search | Routes 2 and 3 together, with route 1's `spec` | Chips 0.1 s after route 1 |
| 5 | 18 s | Sees the ranking | The results stand where Burro was, and nothing above or beside them moves. Ten results in rank order, and "Show 10 more". The map filled by score band, with numbered pins on the first ten. The first five are cards, and hold a line of dashes where each sentence will go. The live region says how many areas were ranked and names the first | Route 6 for each of the first five, once route 2 has answered | List and map 0.4 s after route 1, and no sooner than the wait, which is 3.75 s from the key press. While the wait was 2 s they were 2.5 s typical and 4 s at worst |
| 6 | 17 to 28 s | Reads the first card | The rank on its pennant, the name, the borough and the fit, with "approx data" beside the fit where it is not whole. In its heading, the way to compare: "Add to compare". A line for each thing that was asked for: its small drawing and its name, and its gauge with a picture at each end. The trade-off, in one sentence, on a band of its own. Then "Show the working" and "More like this". The working is one press away, and holds where the area is, why it fits, the source of the trade-off, the figures behind each vibe, what has no figure, each journey, the cost range and how the fit is worked out, each with the key of its source. Until 2026-09-27 the card held one reason too, words beside each gauge, and the key of a source after each sentence | None | Sentences 0.3 s after the list. The cost 0.5 s after, in the working |
| 7 | 28 to 30 s | Changes one thing, in a chip or a control | The list stays where it is, at full strength, under a line that says "Working out the ranking again". The control shows its new value at once | Route 2 with the spec and one edit, then route 3 | 150 ms typical, 400 ms at worst |

There are two layouts, and in each the order of the page is the order it is drawn in. On a wide screen the map stands beside what is read: beside the two tabs before a search, and beside the answer after one, where it stays in sight while the answer scrolls. `--columns` in the tokens says how wide the two are. On a narrow screen the page is one column: the first result stands directly after what Burro understood, whole on the first screen, and after it the fold of the settings, the way to sharing, a strip of the map with the whole map one press away, and the rest of the results. The two tabs are for beginning a search. Once one is open there is no tab: the list, the map and the way to the table are on one page at every width, so nothing is behind a tab when the answer comes. **Until 2026-09-26 there were three layouts.** From 80rem the settings stood open at the left of the answer, in a column of 17rem, and the map in another at its right, which at 1440 was 272 px, 624 and 272. The founder walked it, and asked for the map larger and the settings folded ([ADR 0035](../adr/0035-the-search-page-is-one-box-and-the-look-gives-way-to-the-answer.md), as amended).

- **The answer comes first, and on a phone the first result is whole on the first screen.** It was not: after a search the first screen of a phone held the banner, the header, the title, the box, a line of two, the chips, two buttons, a strip of map and two more buttons, and the first result began at 750 px and ended at 1,110 on a screen of 844. What stood above it was cut. Once a search is open the title takes no room, and is kept as the heading of the page for whoever hears it. On a narrow screen the banner is one line, "This is made-up test data.", and says all of it before a search and on every other page. What happened is one short line, which as it was first written read "22 areas ranked. What you asked for counts most." Since 2026-09-26 it says what the order means in a sentence, in short so that it is two lines of a phone, and in full where one line chooses (`MEANS` in `StatusLine.tsx`): "22 areas ranked. The closest match to what you asked for comes first." Where a journey or a budget counts for more than anything that was asked of the place, the line says that in its place: "21 areas ranked. The journey and the budget count the most here." A person who asked for leafy and quiet and named a workplace is first shown the areas nearest the workplace, and must not read the first result as the leafiest. It is said of every ranking it is true of, and the chips, once they are opened out, say where to change it (`src/lib/search/leads.ts`). The name of the first result is said to a screen reader and not drawn, because its card stands directly under the line. The heading "Results" takes no room. Sharing and the map stand after the first result. So does the fold of the settings on a narrow screen, "Refine search", closed: on a wide screen it is drawn where it comes in the page, before the first result.
- **The list of results is drawn in two parts, with the map between them.** The first result is one list, and the rest another that says it begins at the second. **The order of the page is the order it is drawn in, which differs by screen.** On a wide screen: the form, the fold of the settings, the first result, the way to sharing, the map, the rest. On a narrow screen: the form, the first result, the fold, the way to sharing, the map, the rest. Until the page was mended on 2026-09-26 it was one order at every width, and on a narrow screen the fold was drawn after the first result and came before it in the page: seen on a phone by keyboard, the keys went 610 px down the screen from the last chip to "Refine search" and 477 px back up to the name of the first result. The style sheet says which screen there is, in the custom property `--screen` of `SearchApp.module.css`, and `SearchApp/order.ts` says the order for each. On a wide screen the map is laid out beside them all: it still comes after the first result in the page, so a keyboard and a screen reader reach the box, what Burro understood and the first result before it. Before a search, where the page is one column, they reach the map first, with a link that skips it. The map is the same map before a search and after one, and it is not drawn again when the results come.

- **The banner** is site copy, shown on every page while `meta.synthetic` is true, at build or in any answer since: "This is made-up test data. The city, its places and every figure are invented. Nothing here describes a real place." It cannot be closed.
- **The preview banner** is site copy too, shown on every page built on a release whose `meta.preview` is true: "This is a preview for the people who build Burro. It is not finished: what it has not measured is missing, and nothing in it has been approved for the public." It cannot be closed. It says nothing of whether the figures are made up, so a preview of real data shows it alone and a made-up preview shows both. It is shown if the release the page was built on is a preview, and as soon as any answer says its release is one, by the body or by the header `X-Burro-Preview`. Then it stays. On a narrow screen, once a search is open, it is one line, "This is a preview.", as the banner of made-up data is: on a phone it was six lines, 138 px of a screen of 844, above the answer. Before a search, and on every other page, it says all of it.
- **A page is shown only while every answer is of the kind of data it was built on.** A page is built on one release and may be read while the service holds another. If an answer says its data is made up and the page was built on data that is not, or the other way, or one is a preview and the other is not, the page gives way to a notice: "This page cannot be shown. This page was built on one kind of data, and the service now answers with another: one of them is made up or unfinished, and the other is not. Nothing is shown, so that no figure is read under the wrong notice. The website must be built again on the release the service holds." So no real figure is read under "made-up", and no made-up figure as real. A release that moved on to another of the same kind changes nothing here: section 5 says what the search does then.
- **Nothing beside the box says how words are handled.** Until the evening of 2026-09-26 a line under the box said two things, in the panel of Quick search. The first was the website's own: "What you type is sent to Burro to be read, and Burro does not keep it." The second was the service's: `reader.notice` of route 11, drawn as it was served, with a link to `reader.terms_url` where there is one, which says whose language model reads what is typed, what goes with it, that nothing private should be typed and that Burro itself keeps nothing of it, or that nothing typed is sent to a language model. Once a search was open they gave way to a link beside the label of the box, "How your words are handled". The founder walked the website a second time, and asked for all three to go. **Both sentences stand on Methods, under "How your words are handled"**, as they did, with one more that says the notice is what the service said when the page was built. The foot of every page leads there, by the word "Privacy", and to whoever hears the page by that word and the heading it leads to. The website still writes no provider's name and no terms of its own, and a test reads its source for both (ADR 0019). **The page still asks the service who reads as it opens, and sends no sentence until the service has said** (`flow.loadReader`): nothing by the box says that it is waiting. What it gives up, and what holds it, is in [ADR 0023](../adr/0023-what-is-typed-goes-as-typed-and-people-are-told.md), as amended, and the legal drafts say what it changes of what they promise.
- **In the row a chip says every part of itself, and marks each part that nobody said**, since 2026-09-27: "Cindermoor Works, public transport assumed, 40 minutes, flexible assumed". Until then a chip folded two parts that nobody said into "Burro assumed the rest", and did not say whether a limit is firm until it was pressed, though that decides which areas are left out. It is built both ways, and `IN_THE_ROW` in `ChipRow/says.ts` chooses: `each`, which it is on, or `rest`, which folds the parts nobody said as it did, where there are two of them or more. Whichever is chosen the row says whether a limit is firm, and names a kind of house that was assumed. Where Burro took a terraced house for "a house", the kind is named with the word "assumed" on it, whatever else was assumed: "£600,000, a terraced house assumed, flexible assumed". It is what the budget is held against, and nobody said it. A part of a chip follows a comma, so it begins with a small letter: how a journey is made and the kind of a home are written to begin a line, as they do where they are chosen, and in a chip a capital stood in the middle of the line (`afterAComma` in `src/lib/search/chips.ts`). After one press the website marks it from the edit that holds the kind, which says it is Burro's.
- **The examples** hold no proper noun, so none can go stale when a release changes. They are for a first search. Once a search is open they go, because an example pressed then would be added to it: "Buying a terraced house" on top of "leafy and quiet". They come back with "Start again".
- **What Burro says of a search stands directly under the box.** In a browser, pressing Search changed nothing on screen: on a phone what was understood began a screen and a half down, under the examples, the tenure and the place field. Now nothing stands between the box and what Burro says. Section 4.1 gives where each part was measured to stand. Before a search the page is the heading, the two tabs and the panel of the one that is chosen. It holds no chips: nothing has been understood of anything.
- **Once a search is open the box says that what is typed is added to it**: its label reads "Add to this search", and "Start again" stands beside Search. It empties the box, puts the focus in it, and goes back to the page as it first stood, with the defaults the API served. Nothing of the search before is left: the tray of areas to compare is emptied, and no area is chosen on the map.
- **What is added to a search is what is sent.** The box keeps what it held, so that the words an offer rests on can be shown in it, and what is typed next is typed after them. It sent all that it held, with the search as it stood, and the first sentence was read a second time, onto a search that had been set by hand since. It now sends what stands in the box after the words the search has read, and never those words again. Section 3 says what is sent in every case and what a person is told, and section 10 what is kept to know it: counts, and no word. Words that were read and come back into the box, as when the keys that undo put back what was taken out, or when a copy of them is pasted, are no addition, and are not sent again.
- **What goes when a search opens hands the focus on.** A word of the shelf that is added, and a place picked from the field, open the search, and the tabs go with it, with the helper that held the shelf and the panel that held the field. The focus goes to what Burro understood, and is never left on nothing.
- **A search begins one of two ways, and the page says so.** The first screen once held the box, the shelf standing open, renting or buying, the field, three examples and the way to the settings: seven things asked to be used first. On the morning of 2026-09-26 it was made one box, with everything else behind three helpers, "Try an example", "Start from a word" and "Choose without typing". The founder walked it, and wrote that the first page lacked a narrative: a search is a text search or a search by the settings, and it was not clear that a person can go down either path. So under the heading stand two tabs. "Quick search" is chosen at first, and holds the box with its two helpers. "Deep search" held what the third helper opened, renting or buying, the field and the settings, with the button that ranks by them: that helper is gone. Since the page was mended it asks each thing once, in the settings, and the button alone makes a search of them (section 3). They are tabs as a browser and a screen reader know them: one list of tabs, each a native button that says whether it is chosen and names the panel it shows. The arrow keys move between them and go round at either end, Home and End go to the first and the last, and the tab a key leads to is chosen as it takes the focus. Tab goes from the chosen tab into its panel, since the chosen tab is the only one a keyboard stops at. The chosen tab stands forward, on the cream of its panel and joined to it, with a band of amber at its head, and the other stands back on sand: that a tab is chosen is said by more than its colour. The button is of one size whichever is chosen, and nothing moves under a press. **What a panel holds stays on the page while the other is chosen**, hidden and as it was left: so what was typed and what was set are there when a person comes back, and nothing of either is kept in an address or in the storage of the browser. A helper is a native button that says whether it is open. It opens what was there, as it was, under the whole line, so that nothing above it moves. The helper that is pressed takes the focus where it stands, and the page is not moved under the press. Escape closes what is open and gives its button the focus. After a search the tabs and the helpers go, and come back with "Start again", with Quick search chosen and each helper closed. A helper the release gives nothing to offer is not drawn: no example it can answer the whole of, or no vibe any area is placed on. What a helper opens says its name once: the shelf is named by the helper that opens it and draws no heading there, and the heading of the examples is kept for whoever hears the page and is not drawn.
- **In a window that is wide and not high, what begins a search stands whole on the first screen.** Measured at 1280 by 720 of the page as it was, with three helpers under one box: the helpers stood from 706 to 757, at the very foot of the first screen, and 37 px under it. What stood over them gave way there, in the order the look gives way in, and last the drawing beside the heading, which was drawn as on a phone. They then stood from 654 to 705. It is said of a window under 768 px high and from 60rem wide, before a search, and of no other. Measured again on 2026-09-26, with two tabs over the box: the field stands from 579.5 to 652.5, on the first screen, and the two helpers from 694.5 to 738.5, so that their foot is 18.5 px under it.
- **With scripts off no tab and no helper is drawn, and what they hold is in the page.** A tab and a helper each need a script, and a button that could open nothing is not drawn where the browser says it runs no script. The page as it is built stands both panels one under the other, each under its name, with an example, a word and the field in them.
- **Before a search the settings are the panel of Deep search, and after one they are the fold named "Refine search".** They are one part, `SettingsPanel`, which the page places. Until 2026-09-26 they were the last thing the last helper opened under 80rem, and stood open beside the answer from it.
- **While a search is read, Burro hops where the results will stand.** The page drew a row of grey bars and five grey cards. In their place is one box, as high as the result that stood there, and as high as a first result with what Burro understood over it where none did, as each was before a result came to hold less: section 4.1 has what was measured of it since. Until the evening of 2026-09-26 he was seen while a sentence was read, and no longer: on a quick service that was a few frames. The founder asked that he is seen: "even if not needed, create a few seconds load time to allow the bunny jumping into a hole animation to appear and take center stage". So a search that begins with no result in sight is shown no sooner than he has gone down his hole and come up twice. Measured in a browser on 2026-09-26: he was seen for under half a second before, and a first result is shown 2.2 to 2.4 seconds after the press since. Where less movement is asked for it is shown an eighth of a second after the press. One number said how long, `ROUNDS` in `src/lib/search/wait.ts`, and a round was as long as one round of his hop, `--motion-hop`, which was a second: so where the system asks for less movement, and that token is nought, nothing is waited for. **The founder walked the website a third time, and asked for more of him**: "slow the hole hopping animation down a bit and make it last a tad longer, make soil flick up as it burros". Since 2026-09-27 a round is a second and a quarter, in twelve frames where there were eleven, and a first search waits for three rounds, which is three seconds and three quarters. Two numbers say how long, `ROUNDS` and `--motion-hop`, and the wait is the one times the other: no length of time is written into the wait. Soil flicks up from his hole as he comes up and as he goes down, inside his box, and nothing on the page moves because of it. What was timed above is of the wait as it was. Timed again on 2026-09-27, in Chrome, on a service that answered in under 40 thousandths of a second: a first result was shown 3.82 seconds after the press at both sizes, where it was shown after 2.07, and 93 thousandths of a second after it where less movement was asked for. A search that is refined, with its results in sight, waits for nothing. That a least time on stage was weighed that morning and put aside is in [ADR 0035](../adr/0035-the-search-page-is-one-box-and-the-look-gives-way-to-the-answer.md). He and the words under him are kept from a screen reader, which hears the same words from the line under the box. What stood in the place of the first result is kept and hidden while he hops, and not taken away: taken away, a working that a person had opened would be closed when the same area came first again. He hops there too while a first ranking that a control asked for is worked out: the settings ranked as they stand, and a word of the shelf. A person waits there for a first answer as they do after a sentence. Seen in a browser, the wait was drawn as dashes, under the card of the word, out of sight, and the search opened only when the answer was in. The search is now open from that press, as it is from the press that sends a sentence. Nothing is read there, so neither he nor the line under the box says that a search is: both say "Working out the ranking". He does not hop while a ranking is worked out again: the list stays in sight, and says so.
- **As a first search opens of a sentence, the field stays where it stood in the window.** The heading over the box gives way as the search opens, and everything under it would rise by as much under a person who is looking at the box. The page measures the field at the press, and moves itself by what the field moved.
- **The browser holds the page by what is read, and never by the ground.** A browser holds what is in sight where it is while what stands over it grows, by the first thing in sight. The meadow is the first thing in the page and is always in sight, so the page was held by the ground, which never moves, and so by nothing. Measured at 390 by 844 on a long sentence, after its one button: the chips grew from two to six, and the first result stood from 552 to 964, where on the trunk it stood from 303 to 680. The ground now says `overflow-anchor: none`.

On a wide screen, before a search:

```
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Banner: this data is made up                                             |
| +----------------------------------------------------------------------+ |
| | Burro, and the rabbit                        Vibes  Methods  Sources | |
| +----------------------------------------------------------------------+ |
| +--------------------------------------+ +---------------------------+   |
| | Find your area      Burro, who moves | | Map: every area, in one   |   |
| | Who Burro is for, in a sentence      | | plain colour              |   |
| +--------------------------------------+ |                           |   |
| [ Quick search ][ Deep search ]          |                           |   |
| +--------------------------------------+ |                           |   |
| | Tell me what's important in your ... | | zoom buttons, legend      |   |
| | What a useful sentence holds         | | [Table of all areas]      |   |
| | Prompt box                  [Search] | +---------------------------+   |
| | [Try an example] [Start from a word] |                                 |
| +--------------------------------------+                                 |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

And after one:

```
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Banner: this data is made up                                             |
| +----------------------------------------------------------------------+ |
| | Burro, and the rabbit                        Vibes  Methods  Sources | |
| +----------------------------------------------------------------------+ |
| +--------------------------------------+ +---------------------------+   |
| | Prompt box           [Again][Search] | | Map: areas, pins 1 to 10, |   |
| | What happened, and any notice        | | the names of areas        |   |
| | Chips, which wrap                    | |                           |   |
| | What was left out, in one line       | |                           |   |
| +--------------------------------------+ | zoom buttons, legend      |   |
| [ Refine search   v ]  Areas can be     | | [Table of all areas]      |   |
|                        compared         | +---------------------------+   |
| +--------------------------------------+   which stays in sight as       |
| | 1  Card, in short   [Add to compare] |   the answer scrolls            |
| +--------------------------------------+                                 |
| [Share]                                                                  |
| | 2  Card, in short ... 6  Row ... 10  Row |                             |
| [Show 10 more]                                                           |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Areas to compare: at the foot of the screen once one is chosen           |
+--------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

No column of settings stands open at any width. On a narrow screen the map stands over the tabs before a search, as a low strip, and after the first result once one is open. Every box stands on the meadow, with grass between one and the next.

## 2. Pages and routes

One environment variable says where the API is: `NEXT_PUBLIC_BURRO_API_URL`. The browser calls that address itself. The website has no server action and no middleware, and one route handler, which passes the routes of accounts on to the API and passes nothing on while accounts are off (section 2.3). So nothing that a person types into a search passes through the website's server. The server calls the same address only while it builds a page, and only routes 4, 5, 6 and 11. With the variable unset a build reads the recorded responses of section 11, which is how a build runs where no API can, and the browser's client answers `not_configured` without sending anything. `src/lib/api/config.ts` reads the variable, `next.config.ts` reads it for the content security policy, and a test fails if any other file does. A second variable, `BURRO_SITE_URL`, says where the website itself is. It is read on the server, in `src/lib/indexing.ts` and nowhere else, and is used for what a search engine is told and for nothing else.

| Path | Page | Rendered on the server | Rendered in the browser | API | Cached |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/` | Search and results | Shell, banner, the heading, the two tabs and the panel of each, which a browser with scripts off is shown one under the other, built from route 11, with the bands of every vibe from route 4 handed to the browser. Its title is its heading and the name of the website, as every page's is. Every area by name, as a link to its page, is in the table, which the browser draws when it is opened. Before a search, a field at the foot of Deep search finds an area by its name: every area that bears the name is a link to its page, under the field, and adds nothing to a search | `SearchApp`: everything in section 3 | Build: 11, 4. Browser: 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9 | Static, rebuilt hourly at most. Every `POST` is sent `no-store` and its answer kept only in the store |
| `/[city]/[area]` | One area | The whole page, from route 6, in this order. The name and the borough, and under them the label its publisher gives the area and what is known of the name: who wrote it, that it is a draft or was checked, and the way to `/methods#names`. Beside them the town of the area, drawn by rule from four of its bands, with its line, and by the town the way to compare the area. The portrait (`portrait`): first what the area is like in short, in five lines at most, and beside it where it is, which is `LocatorMap`, the neighbours and the nearest station; then where the area sits on every vibe, one line each, in the lists the API puts them in. The list of what else the page holds. Then four parts that are closed until they are pressed: the five areas most like it (`similar`), cost by kind of home, every feature grouped by `dimension`, and the sources. Then a fifth, where route 11 says a census is served: its heading and the words that offer it, and no figure (section 2.1). Last, "Go and look": the stations, and what each vibe that is shown cannot see. Under each fact its source and its date are written out. What opens is the browser's own `details`, so the page works with scripts off | The button that adds the area to the compare tray, and the tray. "Search for these vibes". How long it takes to the places of the search that is open (`YourJourneys`), from the ranking the session holds. A part that is closed is opened when a link names it: "More like this" on a result leads to `#alike`. The census figures, which are asked for when their part is opened | Build: 4 for the paths, then 6 for each, 5 and 11. Browser: 13, when the census is opened | Static, one page an area, rebuilt hourly at most. A slug the release lacks is a 404, and so is an area asked for under a city that is not its own |
| `/compare?a=slug&a=slug` | Compare 2 to 4 areas | Shell, and at the head of the page, before anything else, the way back: "Back to your search", which leads to the search as it was left, or "Start a search" where none is open, as where a comparison was opened by its link alone. It stands again at the foot. Then the areas' names from route 4 with a link to each area's page, where each of them sits on each vibe, from the same answer, which the town at the head of each area is drawn from, and the defaults and the vibes from route 11. The server reads the slugs and keeps only those that name an area of the release | `CompareView`: `CompareAreas`, then `CharacterTable`, the vibes of each area side by side (`character`), and then `CompareTable`, the measured parts | Browser: 7, with the spec of the search the session holds. With no search open it sends the default the API served, and says so | Not cached: the page is drawn when it is asked for. Never indexed |
| `/s#share_id` | Open a share | Shell, the form from route 11 and the areas from route 4, as for `/`. The id is in the fragment, which a browser sends to no server, so the page is built once and is the same for every link | `SharedSearch`: it reads the fragment, opens the share, and then shows the search page. Once the share is open the answer comes first, as it does on the search page: the heading "A shared search" gives way to it, and is kept for whoever hears the page. That the search came from a link, what a share holds, a line for `coarsened` and one for `stale` are one press away, in a fold named "A shared search" beside the way to share, after the first result. "Start again" opens the search again as the link holds it, and does not begin an empty one | Build: 11, 4. Browser: 10, then 3 and 6, then whatever a search calls | Static, hourly. Never indexed |
| `/robots.txt`, `/sitemap.xml` | What a crawler is asked, and what there is to find | All of it | None | Build: 11, and 4 for the sitemap | Static, hourly |
| `/vibes` | Every vibe of the release, and the key to every drawing | All of it, from `MetaData.tags`, `.features`, `.families` and `.attributions`, with the bands of route 4 and the boundaries of route 5. **First, the key**, under the name "Key to the drawings" (`Key`, at `/vibes#key`): every drawing and mark that a visitor meets on the website, each at the size it is met, beside a sentence or two that say what it means, laid out by where a drawing is met. The things of a search: the small drawing of each vibe, whose name leads to the vibe, and of what else can be asked for; a thing in outline, which counts for nothing; the gauge, which says how much a thing counts; and the cross that takes a thing out. Where an area sits on a vibe: the gauge, state by state, which is five steps and a peg with the one picture of each end of its vibe (`BandKey`). **Since 2026-09-27 it says what the two pictures of a vibe mean, that neither end is the better one, and what the chequered mark and "approx data" mean.** The two pictures of each vibe stand with the vibe, further down the page, and one line stands every pair in the key as well (`PAIRS_STAND` in `VibesList/look.ts`): the page is then 889 px higher on a phone. The things of a search hold the drawing of each group the settings gather things in, since three of them had none. On a result and across Burro: the rank on its pennant, what stands beside a trade-off, the key that opens a source, the arrow of what opens and closes, and the edge of a notice and of a fault. The key opens with its five parts by name, each a link to its part. On the map: the five greens, land with no match, dots, lines, water and a pin. And what a town is built of (`TownKey`): its trees, the height of its buildings, its lit windows, its roofs, and what a blank means. All of it is in sight, and nothing of the rabbit is in it. A drawing that is met on no page is to be in no row of it: the two marks of the button that stood by the rabbit went from it on the evening of 2026-09-26. **The carrot has its row still.** Until the night of 2026-09-26 it said that Burro asks where it is not sure, and adds nothing until a person chooses, which was untrue of a page that asks nothing. It says where a carrot is still met: beside the choice in hand in a list, as the examples under the box are, and the places that match a name. Beside the cross of a chip the key says what Burro does with a sentence, and under what a note is, what it never takes for a person, and why. A band of amber is said there to mark a note, as the line that names what was left out of a search. Then what is true of every vibe, said once. Then each vibe, laid out as the next is so that one is read against the other, and far shorter than it was: its drawing, its name, what it means in a sentence or two, and the city coloured by it in five bands. Under the map is the gauge of the vibe: the five shades of the map between its two ends, each end with its one picture and its name. Until 2026-09-27 only a vibe that runs between two named ends had them. A vibe whose map is not drawn has its two ends under what it means. Under each vibe, one fold, closed, named for what a person would ask: "How Burro works this out". It holds what stood in sight until 2026-09-26: the recipe in short with how each part is read and the other recipes that hold it, the period and the source of each part, everything the vibe cannot see, and where it is found, band by band. At the foot of the page, one fold more: how an area is placed on a vibe, in site copy, held to the contract by a test. A vibe whose recipe holds recorded crime says so on its face, and that is never folded. Until the evening of 2026-09-26 a vibe that is a rough guide said so beside its name, with why: the founder asked that a visitor is not told it (section 5.2). The page holds no vibe of its own, so it cannot disagree with the engine. A vibe is reached by `/vibes#tag_id` ([ADR 0040](../adr/0040-a-page-that-explains-leads-with-a-key-and-folds-its-working.md)) | None | Build: 11, 4, 5 | Static, hourly |
| `/methods` | Methods | All of it. **First, a short and plain account** of how Burro finds areas for a person, in site copy, which somebody reads in a minute: what it looks at, how it ranks, what it will not do, and that every figure has its source. **Under it, one fold, closed, which holds all of the rest as it was**, and each part of it under the id it had: each `Metric` with its `definition`, `unit`, `vintage` and sources, what a vibe is with the way to `/vibes`, `limits`, `defaults`, engine and release, and how a person's words are handled. The key of a town stood after what a vibe is until 2026-09-26, and stands in the key of the page of vibes alone. In site copy, held to the contract by a test: how an area is named and that a name is a draft, how a journey is timed and how it counts, and what each word for how sure a cost is means. Where a journey of the release is estimated, how it is estimated stands in the place of how a journey is timed, under the same id: the line "This is an estimate that Burro worked out from the distance, and not a time from a timetable." as the API serves it, and every number of the estimate from `journey_estimate` of route 11. A result leads to the last two by `/methods#journeys` and `/methods#confidence`, and the page of an area to the first by `/methods#names`. How words are handled is at `/methods#words`: until the evening of 2026-09-26 the search box led to it, and nothing beside the box does since. The foot of every page leads to it, by the word "Privacy" (`WORDS_IN_THE_FOOT` in `SiteHeader/look.ts`). An address that names what the fold holds opens the fold, and every fold that holds it (`OpenToAddress`) | None | Build: 11 | Static, hourly |
| `/sources` | Data sources and licences | All of it, from `MetaData.attributions`: one list, in one box, with one entry for each `Source`, anchored by `source_id`. In sight: its name, who published it, its licence, the way to the publisher, and its credit, as the API gives it, with what its publisher asks to be said wherever the credit is shown. Folded under it: what Burro uses the source for, which is the features that name it, and when its copy was taken | None | Build: 11 | Static, hourly |
| Not found, error | | Fixed text, under the banner, in one box on the meadow, 40rem wide at the most. A page that is not there has a title of its own, "Page not found", and the drawing of a plot with nothing built on it beside its heading. A fault has a square of poppy, and no drawing: Burro stands beside no failure | "Try again" asks for the page again. The page of a fault stands inside the shell, which holds the search while the tab is open: so on a page that is drawn from that search, which is the search page and the page a shared link opens, the search is begun again first, as "Start again" begins it, with the areas that were chosen from it to compare. Such a page that threw of what its search holds threw again at every press, and only loading the page anew brought it back: seen in a browser, of a ranking whose areas lacked their fields, as a service of another version than the website may send. On any other page the search is kept, to go back to. The button goes with the page of a fault, and hands the focus to `main` | None | |
| A fault in the layout | | | `global-error.tsx`: the same fixed words in a document of its own, in a stated language, with the banner once anything the tab has read said the data was made up. It has no style sheet of its own: the framework has every page fetch ahead what this page is drawn with, and a sheet of its own was fetched by every page, laid by none, and said by the browser in the console of each to be of no use. It is laid out by the sheet of the shell (`Alone`) | None | |

- **There is no statement of accessibility.** Until the evening of 2026-09-26 the website had a page at `/accessibility`, in site copy: what is met, what is checked automatically, what is known to fall short, what has not been tested, and how to report a problem. The founder asked for it to go, and the website makes no claim of a standard since ([ADR 0042](../adr/0042-the-website-makes-no-claim-of-a-standard-of-accessibility.md)). The address is a page that is not there. Section 9 is what the website is built to, for whoever changes it, and says where it is known to fall short.
- `[city]` is `synthetic` for ids that begin `syn` and `london` for `lon`. `src/lib/city.ts` holds the pair and nothing else does.
- **One rule says whether anything may be indexed**, and it is in `src/lib/indexing.ts`: a page may be indexed only when the release is real, is no preview, and the website knows its own address. The address comes from `BURRO_SITE_URL`, read on the server and in that one file. While the rule says no, every page carries `noindex, nofollow`, every answer carries the header `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow`, the robots file names no sitemap, the sitemap lists nothing, and an area's page has no canonical address and no structured data. **The robots file never keeps a crawler out.** One that is kept out never fetches a page, so it never reads that the page asks to be left out, and may still list the address of a made-up place from a link it found elsewhere. The header says the same for what is not a page and can carry no `robots` of its own, such as the robots file and the icon. It is worked out when the website is built, from the same rule, and a release that cannot be read then is taken for one that may not be indexed. When it says yes, every page may be read, the sitemap lists the search, the three pages about the website and one page for each area from route 4, and an area's page carries its canonical address and its structured data. A comparison and the page a share opens are one person's: they carry `noindex`, in the page and in the header, whatever the rule says, and the sitemap never lists them. A test asks all of these at once and fails if any two disagree.
- **What an area's page tells a search engine is what its `area` fact says**, and no more. The title is the name and the borough. The description names them and then describes the page, not the place. The structured data is a `Place` with its name, its address on the website, and the borough as the place that holds it. It holds no figure and no coordinates: no fact holds where an area is. It is written into the page as the text of a script, with every `<` written as its escape.
- "Hourly" is a revalidation of 3,600 seconds: a built page is built again each hour. The API tells a browser to ask each time whether an answer of routes 4, 5, 6 and 11 still stands (`Cache-Control: no-cache`, with the release in the `ETag`), so a browser no longer answers one from its own cache for an hour (section 13, gap 15, met). If an answer in the browser names a `release_id` other than the one the page was built on, the browser fetches routes 4, 5 and 11 again and uses those. It does so beside the search and never in its way: reading the form again changes the form and the names of the areas, and takes nothing from the cards. It is asked for once at a time. If it cannot be read, or the boundaries cannot, the next answer that names the release asks again. Boundaries that could not be read as the page opened are owed in the same way: the next answer asks for them again, with the form, and so does the connection as it comes back. So a map that one slow answer kept from a page is drawn as soon as a person searches, and the page is not loaded anew for it.
- A share is made in `SharePanel`, which opens in place beside the settings once there is a ranking. It says what the link will hold, offers "Share the exact places" unticked, sends route 9 with the spec the store holds, and shows the link `/s#share_id` in a field to copy from, with the stored spec as chips. If `coarsened` is true it says the ranking may differ a little. If nothing was replaced and the box was left unticked, it says that no place needed replacing, because each is a station or a district already: it said "The link holds the places as you named them", which read as if the choice had been ignored. A link is shown only for the search it was made of: change the search, and the link goes. The session keeps the link that was last made, so that it is still there when the person comes back from the page of an area.

What building the area page, the comparison and sharing settled:

- **The session.** The search that is open and the areas chosen to compare are held by `SessionProvider`, which the shell of every page holds. The shell stays while one page gives way to the next, so both are still there after a link inside the website is followed. Loading a page afresh, or closing the tab, loses both. Nothing is written anywhere: section 10 stands.
- **The areas to compare are not part of the search.** They are chosen from an area's page as well as from a result, and an area's page holds no search. The session holds them as each area's id, slug and name. "Start again" empties them: in a browser the tray still held three areas of the search before, under a page that otherwise stood as it first did.
- **The address of a comparison decides what is compared.** The tray builds the address, and the page puts the areas of its address in the tray, so going back to choose others starts from what was compared.
- **A comparison shows a fact's slots and never a cell's numbers.** A cell of route 7 holds `value`, `percentile`, `utility` and `contribution` as numbers. The page shows the slots of the fact the cell cites, as the API formatted them, and the contribution as "Adds 23 of 100 to the fit", rounded down. It never shows `value`, which is the same figure unformatted, nor `percentile`, nor `utility`.
- **Where an area stands is shown only from a search.** Route 7 gives no score. When a search is open and its ranking is of the spec that is sent, each ranked area shows its rank and its fit from that ranking. With no search open it shows neither.
- **The table is stacked on a narrow screen.** Under 60rem each row becomes a block: the thing that counts, then each area under its own name. It is one table in the page either way, and every row and cell says its role, so that a browser which forgets a table when it is laid out as blocks is told again.
- **An area's page gives the source under each fact, not behind a button.** `SourceLine` writes out what `SourceNote` opens, because the page must read with scripts off. Under what the area is like in short they are written out too, and are one press away: five lines of a build of a real city rest on thirteen sources and seven dates, which stood a screen and a quarter of a phone high between what the area is like and where it is. What opens them is the browser's own `details`, so they open with scripts off. Under a vibe the line begins with the API's own words, "Burro's recipe. Made from data published by:", from the slot `made_from`: the sources of a vibe are those of its parts.
- **The portrait opens with what the area is like, in five lines.** A person who has never been to the city opened the page of an area and read "band 4 of 5" eleven times. Under the heading "In short" the portrait now says what sets the area apart from the others of the release: what it has most of, where it sits between two ends, and what it has least of. Each line is one vibe the API placed: its name, where the area sits on it in words a person would use, and a figure of one of its parts. The vibes furthest from the middle band are chosen first, a kind at a time, so that what an area has least of is never crowded out by what it has most of (`inShort` in `src/lib/area/portrait.ts`). A vibe in the middle band is in no line of it, nor is a mixed area, which sits at no one point. Nor is a vibe whose recipe holds recorded crime: recorded crime counts only when a person asks for it, and nobody asked for the summary. The lines end in the source and the date of the facts they are from.
- **A band is said in words a person would use, beside the band itself.** "Among the most", "on the high side", "around the middle", "on the low side" and "among the least" for a vibe that runs one way, since the words were written again on 2026-09-26: each ended in "here", and three people who walked the website asked what "here" was. The key to the drawings says once that an area sits among all the areas that were compared. And "at the Flats end", "towards Flats" and "between Houses and Flats" for a scale, by the names the API gives its ends. They are site copy for a code, as "within" and "over" are, and are in `src/content/bands.ts`. Each is true of the band as the contract counts it, by the areas strictly below, and none says how many sit above, which a band does not say where areas are level. A test holds each to the contract's formula. None is the heading of a list of the portrait: "More of these than most areas" heads the API's list, which is filled by a rule of its own. The band itself, "band 4 of 5", stands beside the words wherever a vibe is drawn on its line.
- **The figure beside a vibe is one a person can picture, and never reads against its band.** Of the parts of its recipe whose unit is a walk in minutes or metres, a count or a share, it is the one that sits nearest the band of the vibe, and of two as near the heavier. A part that the recipe reads from its low end is turned round first. It was the heaviest, whatever it said: "Quiet streets: among the most here" stood beside 27% of homes near a main road, where an area "around the middle" showed 22%. Where the recipe holds none, it is the one the API names (`figure_fact_id`), which is the heaviest of all. So Food and drink says a count of places within a walk, where it said a count for each square kilometre. It is always the fact of a part of that vibe, as the API sent it, and is never a figure of recorded crime.
- **Where the area is stands beside what it is like.** The picture, the areas next to it, each a link to its page, and the nearest station in the contract's own sentence, with its source and date. Under them, once a search is open that names a place, how long it takes from the area to each place, and whether that is within the limit the person set. The names are the ones the search's answer gave, and the times are the ranking's: the page asks the API for nothing, and puts nothing of the search in an address. With no search open, or one that names no place, nothing is said of journeys. Where the ranking in hand does not hold the area, the page says so and gives no time (section 13, gap 32).
- **A band that rests on part of a recipe says so beside it.** A band worked out from two parts of three was shown as any other. Since 2026-09-27 the line of the vibe says so as a result does, in the two words "approx data" after the mark of what is not whole, and the step its peg stands on is chequered. The page of an area has the room to say what the band rests on as well, and says it after the two words where its list is 46rem wide or wider. On a narrower one, as on a phone, it is one press away, under "Details". What an area is like in short keeps to the two words. Where an area has no figure for a part, the line of the vibe says so after the band, in the API's own clause, word for word: "Burro has a figure for 2 of the 3 measurements that go into this vibe, and they count for 75 of 100 in it.", which until the words of the service were written again was "Worked out from 2 of its 3 parts, 75 of 100 by weight." It is a slot of the vibe's fact (`partly`), beside how many parts have a figure, how many there are, and what they carry of the recipe (`known`, `parts`, `share`). What the line opens says the clause again, and then which parts have none, by the names the API gives them. A part the release does not carry has no name to give, and is counted. What the area is like in short keeps to the two counts, "from 2 of its 3 measurements", so that a line of it stays short. `FactRow` gives the counts and the share in a vibe's row wherever it is drawn: on the page of an area, in a result and in the working of a fit. The website adds up no share of its own: a fact that holds no clause, as one recorded before engine 1.5.0 does not, is said from the two counts alone.
- **Every vibe then has its line.** Which vibe stands in which list, and in what order, is the API's (`portrait`): the scales, "More of these than most areas", "Less of these than most areas", "The other vibes" and "Vibes Burro could not work out". A vibe is one line: its name, a mark on a line of five steps between its two ends, where it sits in words, and its band. It is never a colour alone, a percentage, a score or a rank. A scale has no figure beside it on its line. The whole line opens what the vibe is made of: what it means, where its band came from, and every part of its recipe with its share, how it is read, the area's figure, its band, its source and its date. A part with no figure says so. A part the release does not carry is said to be missing, and is never shown by its code.
- **What Burro cannot place is said once, with why.** The page of an area the release holds little of listed ten vibes, each with the same line. Now the vibes that cannot place the area are named in one line, under "Vibes Burro could not work out", and why is said once. What is known of each is one press away, in one part that opens: how many parts of its recipe have a figure, of how many, which have none, and the source. What the area is like in short says how many vibes cannot place it, and leads to why. None has a line, a cell or a band, and none is put in the middle.
- **What is long is one press away.** The areas most like the area, cost, every measured feature and the sources are each closed until pressed. Each is the browser's own `details`, opened by its heading, so it opens with scripts off, and a link that names it opens it (`OpenAtLink`).
- **An area's page ends with "Go and look".** It holds what the API gives that a person can go and see: the stations, the nearest first, each a fact with its source and date, and what each vibe that is shown cannot see, word for word. What every vibe cannot see is said once, in sight. What each vibe cannot see is one press away, under "What each vibe cannot see", with the way to the page of vibes: of fourteen vibes it was two screens of a phone. A vibe that cannot place the area says nothing there. The town centre, the nearest large park and a walk between them are not served, and are not shown (section 13, gap 27).
- **"More like this" is closed until it is pressed.** It lists the five areas the API names, in the order the API gives them, which is the most alike first. Each is its name, which is one press from its page, and one sentence: the contract's own template for a likeness, filled from the slots of the fact (`src/content/templates.ts`, held to the contract by a test; section 13, gap 29). Under it, in words, are the vibes both areas sit in the same band on, by the names the API gives them and from the bands of route 4. A vibe whose recipe holds recorded crime is never among them. The words over the list say the order as the contract gives it: the areas in the same band as this one on the most measures come first. Under the engine before 1.5.0 the counts beside them read 10, 10, 5, 7 and 8, because the order was by a distance that no page shows. The engine now orders them by the count each sentence gives, and the page keeps the order of the answer and sorts nothing. A test holds the counts of every area to that order. Under each is the way to the comparison of the two, where their vibes stand side by side. It came with the page, so pressing it sends nothing. From a result it is a link to this part of the area's page, which holds the slug of the area and nothing of the search.
- **"Search for these vibes" starts a search from an area.** It stands under "More of these than most areas", and says which vibes it adds. It is a link to the search page. Pressing it tells the session which edits to add, one `TagEdit` for each vibe, and the search page sends them to route 2 when it is drawn, as a word of the shelf is sent. Nothing is put in the address, and the request names vibes alone: not the area, and nothing a person typed. With scripts off it leads to the search page and adds nothing.
- **The vibes of each area come first in a comparison.** One row for each vibe the API compares (`character`), in its order, and one column for each area. **Since 2026-09-27 the cell of an area is its gauge**: five steps with a peg, between the one picture of each end of the vibe, and nothing under it. Where the area sits is said in words by the name of the picture, to whoever hears the page. A band that rests on part of what its vibe needs says "approx data" after the mark, and what it rests on is on the page of the area. At the head of its row stand the small drawing of the vibe, its name, which way its bands run, and its source. Until then a cell held where the area sits in words a person would use, and the band. Two lines of `CompareTable/look.ts` choose: `UNDER_A_GAUGE` brings the words back under every gauge, and `ENDS_STAND` stands the two pictures once, at the head of the row, over the names of the two ends. What Burro cannot place an area on is said once, over the vibes, with why: "Burro cannot place Otterby Fields on 10 of the 11 vibes." A cell of such a vibe says "Not placed", and holds nothing to press. The measured parts follow under "What counts".
- **A comparison has one row for each journey, with the same destination across the row.** With two places in a search, the one row "Journey" showed Cindermoor Works for one area beside Wexmoor University for the next, and a journey with no time was not shown at all. The API now sends one row for each journey, in the order of the search, and the page draws each as it comes (`src/lib/compare/rows.ts`). A row is a journey where it names a place (`CompareRow.place`), and is named "To Cindermoor Works" under the API's own label. Every area has a cell in every row: its times, the journey that left it out with the limit it is over, or "No journey time in this data" with the source of the fact that says so. An area that is not ranked is timed all the same, and adds nothing to a fit it does not have. The journeys count as one thing, so each of their rows holds one weight, and it is said once, under the first of them. How much a thing counts is said as a weight, "Weight 100", and one line over the table says what a weight is, and that the weights are not shares and do not add up to 100: "Counts for 100 of 100" stood over "Counts for 10 of 100" and five things at 5 each. Under it too, where there are two journeys or more: which of them counts, in the words of the settings. What the journeys add to the fit stands in the cell of the journey that decided it. Where the average of them counts the API gives that figure for none of them (contract 9.2), and one line says so: "Burro works out what the journeys add to the fit for all of them together, which is why no single journey says its own."
- **A vibe that counts in a search is drawn in its row as a vibe is everywhere**: a mark on its line, where the area sits in words, and the band, with what it adds to the fit and its source. It was laid out as the columns of its fact, which is five lines for a band.
- **A fit that rests on part of what counts says so where the fit is.** Beside the rank and the fit of such an area stand the mark of what is not whole and "approx data", and then how many of the things that count it has a figure for, and that what each thing adds is then a larger share of the fit. Until 2026-09-27 a warning sign in poppy stood there: nothing of a comparison warns. An area the engine does not rank for want of character says so in words (`character_unknown`). A status the website has no words for is left out, and never shown as its code. The vibes are of the release and of no search, so they are compared whatever the search holds, and when nothing is set to count.
- **Every feature of the release has a row on an area's page**, under "What is measured here", which is closed until it is pressed. Each group of it is closed under its own name, in the browser's own `details`: a release of a hundred measures opened to seventeen screens of a desktop, every group at once, and now opens to the names of its ten groups. One the area has no figure for says "No figure in this data", and shows no figure, no comparison and no source.
- **The brands have a heading of their own there, "Brands nearby"**, after shops and services. Its rows are in this order: the mix of brands, which is what is ranked on; what is counted of each tier, the places within reach and how far the nearest is; and then the chains, each by its name, with how far the nearest place of it is. The name of a chain is the API's, in the label of its measure. A chain with no place within 2,000 m says "No figure in this data", as any row does.
- **What is served with no fact behind it is not shown.** Whether a station is step-free, an area's other names, its coordinates, a feature's coverage and a tag's raw score are all in route 6 and in no fact, so none has a source or a date (section 13, gap 9).
- **Cost on an area's page is a row for each kind of home**, as `FactRow` lays a `cost` fact out. `CostRange` and its bar are for a result card, where there is one kind of home and a budget to mark on it.
- **A price that is one number is drawn as one number.** A publisher's own middle price has no range (contract 2.5, `cost_buy_median`). The row and the card show the one figure, say that it is the middle price of homes of that kind of all sizes and which year of sales it is of, and say under it that the publisher gives no range and does not say how many sales it rests on. No range is drawn, nothing stands in for an end, and no word or pip says how sure it is. What the two figures of a range mean is said on an area's page only where a range is shown.
- **A price that was counted from sales says how many.** A build of London works a price out from the sales of three years (contract 2.5, `cost_buy_sold`). It is drawn as one number, as above, with the months the sales are of and how many there were, each a slot of the fact. It does not say that nothing is known of what it rests on. Under either kind of one number stands the API's sentence that about half of the homes sold there went for less.
- **A rent of a wider place is never drawn without the place it is of.** A build of London holds no rent of an area: it holds the rents recorded in the postcode district an area lies in, or in its borough (contract 2.5, `cost_rent_recorded`). The row and the card show the range and the middle, the place the figure is of, the months the rents were recorded in and how many they were, each a slot of the fact, and the API's sentence that the figure is not of this area alone. No word and no pip says how sure it is. What the publisher advises is the API's too, and is said once: over the rents of an area's page, in the offer of a budget to rent, and on Methods, under "How rents are held". The line that counts what a firm budget left out ends in the API's line that each rent is of a postcode district or of a whole borough.
- **A firm budget says what it leaves out.** The words of the offer are the API's: areas where the middle price is more than 25% over the budget are left out. An area that is kept over the budget says how far over its middle price is, and that about half of the homes sold there went for less.
- **Opening a share leaves the search that was open alone until the share has come.** If the link leads nowhere the page says so in the API's words, and the search before is as it was. When the share comes it takes the place of the search whole: no question, no name and no edit of the search before is carried into it.
- **A link that is already open is not opened again.** The store keeps the id of the share a search came from, so coming back to the link does not put the stored spec over what the person has changed since.
- **What follows the `#` is sent only if it is an id.** Anything else is never sent, never shown and never put in a link. The page says the link is not one, in fixed words.
- **A share whose release has moved on reads the form again first**, so that it is drawn with the names and the limits of the release that ranked it. The line that says a share is stale names the release its ranking came from, which the answer names, and not the one the page was built on.

### 2.1 The census figures of an area

An area's page shows census figures about the people who lived there ([ADR 0014](../adr/0014-evidence-first-and-census-figures-shown.md)): age, households, country of birth, ethnic group and religion. They are shown so that they are read as a description and not a verdict. The contract says what is served and what can never reach it (sections 2.10 and 9.6).
