## 5. The controls

### 5.1 The settings that are visible, and the edit each becomes

Every control builds one `Operations` with one edit in it and five empty arrays. `provenance` is `ui_edit`. A field not named below carries its sentinel. Limits come from `MetaData.limits`, labels from `MetaData.features` and `.tags`, so the form never offers what the reducer would refuse.

**The settings are one accordion.** The founder walked the website, where every group was closed at first and the arrow of each stood before its name, and wrote: "for the settings place the arrow to the right of the button and make it clearer that they are collapsable accordion sections , Leave a couple of the accordians open by default to indicate this." So the groups are one stack of bars, of which the first two are open before a search, and none once a search is open. **They are named "Space requirements"**, in the founder's words, since the evening of 2026-09-26, where they were named "Settings": their name stands over them in Deep search and under "Refine search", as a band of ink at the head of their box, and whoever hears the page finds them by it (`NAMED_IN_SIGHT` in `SettingsPanel/look.ts`). **At their head they say nothing but their name.** They said what they are and how a change is ranked, which under the tab of Deep search said a second time what the tab had said: the founder asked for it to go. That one sentence there names what Burro counts in every search is built too, and one line chooses (`SAYS_AT_THEIR_HEAD`). The groups are: "Budget and home", "Journeys", one group for each family of vibes the API serves (`MetaData.families`, in its order), "Brands nearby", "Air and noise", "Recorded crime", and "Hidden areas" while an area is hidden. A family holds one row for each of its vibes, and "Other things that count" for the features of the family that are in no recipe. "Air and noise" holds the features that belong to no family and are not recorded crime: there are two, and slice 1 did not name a group for them. "Brands nearby" holds the mix of brands and a switch for each chain a person may ask to be near, every one off until it is asked for. They are features of a family, and stand apart from it: thirty switches under "Other things that count" would bury the family.

| Matter | What is so |
|---|---|
| A group | A bar from edge to edge of the settings, with the small drawing of its thing and its name at the left and its arrow at the right, and under it what the group holds, while it is open. The whole bar is what is pressed. It is a native button that says whether it is open |
| Its arrow | At the far end of the bar. It points down while the group is closed, at what would open under it, and up while it is open, and turns in one step |
| An open group | Told from a closed one by more than its arrow: its bar is amber, as what is on is in the look. A closed bar stands on cream |
| What a closed group holds | Said on its bar, beside its name, once something in it is set: "Budget and home" says what can be paid, and "Journeys" the place and the longest journey to it. A person then reads what is set without opening anything. It says what a person asked for, in the names the service gives, and then what Burro counts in every search although nobody chose it, as "Counted in every search: ...", before a search too: a result says how many things its fit is based on, and these are the rest of them. With "Visiting" chosen the bar of the first group says so. Where nothing is set and nothing is counted the bar says nothing. It is heard after the name of the button and whether it is open, as what describes it |
| Open at first | Before a search, the first two groups: it is then seen that a group opens and closes. Once a search is open, none: a person has the answer before them and came to change one thing, the bar of every group says what it holds, and the group that is wanted is one press away. That a couple stand open, and that every group that holds something asked for does, are built too, and one line chooses (`OPENS_AT_FIRST` in `SettingsPanel/look.ts`). With a group that is opened, what a vibe is made of stands open where a person asked for a part of it, and where it holds what the bar of its group names as counted in every search (`openAtFirst` in `SettingsPanel/groups.ts`) |
| What stands open follows the search | Where the search changes elsewhere, as when a sentence is read, what stands open follows it. It holds still for what the settings themselves changed, and while the focus is on what a group holds, whatever changed the search: a person who sent a sentence and went straight to the settings to type a budget had the group they were typing in closed by the answer, and the figure went with it |
| What a person opened or closed | Stays so: a group a person closed stays closed, and one they opened stays open, until they start again. It is kept in memory, by the session the page stands in, as the keys of groups and whether each is open: the settings are taken off the page while their fold is closed, and stand in one place before a search and in another after it |
| Where they stand | Before a search in the panel of Deep search, with no button over them, under the one sentence that says what Deep search is. After one, in the fold named "Refine search". They are laid out by the width of their own box, and never by the width of the screen |
| What is pressed in them is held where it stands | A setting that is changed is answered, and the answer changes what stands over the setting: the bar of its group comes to name it, on a line more where the bar is narrow, and once a search is open the results over the settings are ranked again. Measured on a phone: a thing was turned on, and its switch went 21.5 px down from under the press. So the settings keep where what was pressed stood in the window as the press lands, and as the page is drawn again, before it is seen, the page goes by as much as what was pressed went (`useHeldInPlace` in `SettingsPanel/held.ts`). It is held until the answer to the press is drawn, and no longer. Where a search opens of the press, the page holds what was pressed or brings the answer into sight, and the settings ask nothing: asked of both, the page went twice |

Until 2026-09-26 every group was closed at first, so that twelve controls at most were on screen when the settings opened, and from 80rem the settings stood open at the left of the answer, in a column of 272 px at 1440. Counted as the website was mended: with every group closed they hold 10 controls, and with two open 39 at most after a plain search.

**How a control is drawn.** Every control is the native element it was, and does what it did. What is seen is a drawing, and what is moved, heard and given the focus is the element.

| Control | Drawn as |
|---|---|
| The settings | One box of the look. Each group is a bar of the accordion, whose label is the drawing of its thing and its name in the face of names: the purse for money, a journey for journeys, the drawing of each family, and the plain drawing where the service gives no family, as for the brands, the air and noise, and recorded crime |
| A slider | The browser's own range input, clear, laid over a gauge: a row of steps that fill in amber, with the mark of a weight where the filling has come to. It has one step for each press of its plus, which is ten as the service now gives it |
| A scale | One gauge that runs each way from a post in its middle, five steps each way. At each end a button that holds the picture of that end over its name |
| The minus and the plus | The two buttons of a step, each a drawing, which gives way to the drawing of the button pressed. They have no words in them: the sign is in the drawing and the name is what a screen reader hears |
| A number | A field sunk into the page, its figure heavy in the reading face. Why a number was not taken is said in ink, beside an edge of poppy |
| One of a few | Native radio buttons, each drawn as a button: the one that is chosen is amber, with its lamp lit. Renting, buying and visiting stand side by side, each as wide as the others, so that none is the first of them by its size. As many stand in a row as have the room of their words, and what has no room goes under, as wide as the rest: in a box 300 px wide three in a row were 96 px each, and a word was broken in two |
| So or not | A native checkbox, drawn as a square that is ticked in ink on amber. What is on or off is a switch. Until the evening of 2026-09-26 it was drawn as a lever, which stood at one side on sand and at the other on amber, and the founder wrote that it was unclear what the select state of the toggle is: it was not plain which side was on. **It is drawn as a button of the look, as a choice is, with its lamp and the word "On" or "Off".** Off, it is cream and its lamp is clear. On, it is amber and its lamp is lit, as what is on is everywhere in the look. So it is told by its word, by its lamp and by its colour. The checkbox is laid clear over the whole of its face, so that it takes the press, the keys and the ring of the focus. The word is for the eye, and has the room of the longer of the two, so that the switch is of one size on or off. Whoever hears the page hears it of the switch itself. A press on its word, on its lamp or on the name of the thing is a press on the switch |
| What takes a thing away | "Clear the budget" and "Remove" are edged in poppy |
| What is not set, or counts for nothing | The drawing of its thing, in outline. Nothing is dimmed |
| A button that has nothing left to do | The drawing of the button pressed, in the ink it had. It says that it is off |

- **A gauge is never to be taken for the band of five.** Where an area sits on a vibe is five steps with a peg. How much a thing counts is a row of more steps than five, which fill, in amber, and has no peg.
- **A control is drawn at two pixels of the screen to one of its drawing, on every screen.** At three, as every other drawing is from 60rem, a gauge and its two buttons are 327 px wide, which was more than the column of the settings had while they stood beside the answer. `--px-small` is that pixel, and one rule of `SettingsPanel.module.css` sets it.
- **Where the settings are narrow the figure of a slider stands under its gauge**, and the two ends of a scale stand over it, so that the order in which they are read and reached is as it was: the low end, the slider, the high end.

| Setting | Control | Edit |
|---|---|---|
| Renting, buying or visiting | Three radio buttons, in the first group | `BudgetEdit` `set` with `tenure`, which is `rent`, `buy` or `visit` |
| What a visit asks for | Nothing of a price, of bedrooms or of a kind of home: with "Visiting" chosen the budget, whether it is firm, how much it counts and the kind of home are not drawn, in any group, and what was set of them is not sent. The first group is then named for what it does ask, "Renting, buying or visiting": it was named "Budget and home" still, and held neither (`groupsOf`). Nor is a measure whose figure is a sum of money offered to a visit: what homes sold for, and how much that rose over five years and over ten (`isOfAPrice` in `SettingsPanel/groups.ts`). Where one of them counts as "Visiting" is chosen, it is taken off in the same edit. A measure that describes a street and no purchase stays, as homes in the higher council tax bands do. The service itself weighs such a measure for a visit that names one: a visit that comes of a sentence or of a shared link may hold one, and the settings then offer no switch for it. In their place one sentence says why they are not asked for, and what the areas are ranked by: "You are visiting, so Burro does not ask what you can pay or what kind of home you want. It ranks the areas by everything else you choose, such as the places you need to reach and what you want around you." ([ADR 0041](../adr/0041-a-search-may-be-a-visit.md)) | None |
| Budget | Number field with minus and plus buttons, and "Clear" | Typed: `set` with `amount`. Buttons: `nudge` with `down_small` or `up_small`, disabled while there is no amount. Clear: `clear` |
| Size or type of home | Select, with the segments of the tenure | `BudgetEdit` `set` with `segment` |
| Budget is a firm limit | Checkbox | `BudgetEdit` `set` with `strictness` |
| How much the budget counts | `WeightSlider` | `SettingEdit` `set`, `budget_weight`, `value` |
| A place to reach, up to `max_commutes` | `PlaceCombobox` over route 8, in the group "Journeys", before a search and once one is open | `CommuteEdit` `add` with `place_id` |
| An area, by its name | A field of its own at the foot of Deep search, under its button, before a search. It finds areas alone: every area that bears the name is a link to its page, under the field, with its label beside it. `AN_AREA_BY_NAME` in `SearchApp/look.ts` takes it away | None. An area is no place to reach, and adds nothing to the search |
| How you travel there | Three radio buttons | `CommuteEdit` `update` with `mode` |
| Longest journey | Number field with minus and plus buttons, kept within `minutes_min` and the lower of `minutes_max` and the mode's cutoff | Typed: `update` with `max_minutes`. Buttons: `update` with `max_minutes` 0 and `step` |
| Journey is a firm limit | Checkbox | `CommuteEdit` `update` with `strictness` |
| Remove a place | Button | `CommuteEdit` `remove` |
| Which journey counts | Two radio buttons: the slowest, or the average | `SettingEdit` `set`, `commute_combine`, `choice` |
| Which time counts | Two radio buttons: typical, or just missed | `SettingEdit` `set`, `pt_basis`, `choice` |
| How much journeys count | `WeightSlider` | `SettingEdit` `set`, `commute_weight`, `value` |
| One vibe for each `Tag`, in the group of its `family` | One `WeightSlider`. A vibe that runs one way runs from 0 to 100. A scale is one slider with its two ends named, from 100 towards the low end, through the middle, to 100 towards the high end. **The button at each end of the slider of a vibe bears the one picture of that end of the vibe**, since 2026-09-27, with the name of the end under it where it has one. Of a vibe that runs one way the two buttons bore a minus and a plus, and one line has them bear those again (`ENDS_OF_ONE_WAY` in `WeightSlider/look.ts`): the picture at the low end shows little of the thing, and the low end of such a slider makes the vibe count for nothing, which asks for none of it and not for little. The line over the sliders of a group says so, whichever is chosen. A slider that is of no vibe keeps its two signs, and so does one of a vibe whose ends nobody has drawn | Moved off 0: `TagEdit` `set` with `value` and `toward`, `high` or `low`. Brought to 0, or to the middle: `remove` |
| What a vibe is made of | Under each vibe, a small fold that says what it is made of, closed at first: one row for each part of its recipe that the release can rank, with its switch and its slider, and a count of the parts the release does not carry | As a feature, below |
| One feature for each `Metric` with `rankable` true: a part of a recipe under "What goes into it", or under "Other things that count", "Brands nearby", "Air and noise" or "Recorded crime" | Switch and `WeightSlider`, named by `short_label`. Under the switch, which way a figure counts. Two radio buttons, more or fewer, where `polarity` is `either` | Switch on: `WeightEdit` `nudge` `up_large`, so a switch is worth what a word is. Slider: `set` with `value`. Direction: `set` with `direction` and the weight the person last set, or the spec's where they set none. Switch off: `remove` |
| A vibe added from the shelf | "Add to my search" on the card of a word. A scale with no word of its own on the shelf offers one button for each end | `TagEdit` `nudge` `up_large` with `toward`: the vibe's `shelf_toward`, or the end chosen |
| Hidden areas | "Hide this area" on a card, "Show again" in the settings | `AreaEdit` `exclude`, and `clear` |

- Recorded crime is its own group, the last, closed and off at first, with the caveat of contract 7.3 above its switches. Every figure of recorded crime is in it, whatever recipe holds it. No site copy says "safe" or "unsafe".
- **When recorded crime counts is said one way, on every page that speaks of it.** "Burro counts recorded crime in a search only when you ask for it. You can ask for it by name, switch it on in the settings, or ask for a vibe that includes it." It was written again on 2026-09-26, and says what it said: only when it is asked for, and the three ways of asking. The search said crime counts only when named, beside a chip for a vibe whose recipe counts recorded criminal damage. The rule is one constant (`src/content/crime.ts`), and is what the search says where an edit of crime is refused, what the settings say over the switches, and what Methods, the page of vibes, the page of an area and the comparison say. Which vibe holds recorded crime, and which parts of its recipe are of crime, is read from route 11 and named as the API names it: the website writes no vibe down. Where gritty is built from land use alone, no vibe holds it, and the pages say so: the rule is followed by "At the moment, none of the vibes on offer includes recorded crime." wherever it is said with the release in hand, which is where an edit of crime is refused, over the switches of the settings, on the page of an area and on Methods (`ruleIn`). Without it the third way of the rule was true of nothing there. Such a vibe says on its face that it counts recorded crime, with the caveat, on the page of vibes, where its line is opened on the page of an area, and in its row of a comparison. It is in no summary of an area and in no list of what two areas share, and no figure of recorded crime stands beside a vibe.
- In the empty state the choice of renting, buying or visiting sends nothing: it swaps the spec for the one of `defaults` that the API returned for that kind.
- `WeightSlider` is a range input from 0 to 1 in steps of `weight_unit`, shown as 0 to 100. What 0 and 100 mean is drawn where it can be seen: once for each group that holds a slider or the room of one, whether or not a thing of it is on, and under a slider that stands alone, as in a chip. Beside it sit two buttons that move it by `weight_step_small`, and a number field: a minus and a plus, or, of the slider of a vibe, the one picture of each end of the vibe. It sends on release, or 150 ms after the last key or the last press of a button, never while it is dragged. A number typed in is sent when the field is left, or on Enter.
- **A scale is one slider with two named ends.** It runs from 100 towards one end to 100 towards the other, and rests in the middle, which counts for nothing. At each end is a button named for the end, "Going out, towards Calm", which moves it a step that way. Where it stands is said in words beside it and to a screen reader: "50 towards Calm". It has no number field, because a number does not say which end. Under it one line says what the middle and the ends mean.
- A typed budget or journey time is sent when the field is left, or on Enter, and never key by key: half a number is not a number. A budget is sent as typed, so that the API can say it is out of range. A journey time is brought within the limits first.
- A control that sets a value shows it at once. A minus or plus button on the budget or the journey time sends a step, and the field shows where the API says it landed: the website does not work the step out for itself.
- **A switch that is off offers no slider, and keeps the room of one.** Until the evening of 2026-09-26 the slider came when the switch was turned on, and the two ways where either can count with it, and all that stood under them went down the page by as much: the founder wrote that there was too much layout shift when toggling. So each is drawn at rest while the thing is off, made as it is made while it is on and so of the same size (`SliderAtRest`). At rest it is a drawing: it offers nothing, and says nothing to whoever hears the page. Turned on, the slider stands where its drawing stood. A thing that counts one way keeps no room for a choice it never asks. What the scale of a slider means is said at the head of a group, whether or not a thing of it is on. **It costs a group its length**: a group is as long as if every thing of it were on. At 1440 by 900 the seven other things that count of the first family were 1,512 px high with each slider under its thing, and 1,240 with each beside it. On a phone such a group was 1,882 px, where it was about 550 with everything off. **Measured on 2026-09-26, of the seven things of going out and food**: the group is 1,410 px high at 1440 by 900 and 2,066 at 390 by 844. A thing that was switched on said "On" where it said "Off", its slider stood where its drawing at rest stood, and nothing on the page moved or changed size, at either size. On a phone the bar of the group is a line higher once it says that a thing of it is not counted, 106 px where it was 86: what was pressed is held where it stood in the window, and the page goes 20 px under it. So in a box 34rem wide or wider the slider of a thing stands beside its switch and its name, at the far side of the row, with its two ways under it there, and every slider of a group stands in one column. In a narrower box, as on a phone, it stands under its thing. `SLIDER_STANDS` in `SettingsPanel/look.ts` chooses.
- **A feature is named plainly, and says which way a figure counts.** A switch is named by the API's `short_label`, which says the wish where there is one way to wish, "Less transport noise", and the measure where either way may be wanted, "Pubs and bars" (section 13, gap 16, met). Methods names a feature by its `label`, which is the publisher's. Under each switch the form says which way a figure counts, as "A higher figure ranks an area higher", in the words Methods uses for `polarity`, tied to the switch as its description. The two ways a person chooses between are "A higher figure ranks higher" and "A lower figure ranks higher". No way is called the better: until the words were written again on 2026-09-26 the form said "A higher figure counts as better". The settings must stand alone, because they are what a person uses when their words cannot be read.
- **A part the release does not carry is named as the API names it, and never by its code.** A recipe may hold a part the release has no figure for. Route 11 names each (`recipes[].waits_on`), and the shelf, the settings, the page of vibes and the page of an area say it by that name, with "Not in this data yet". Where the API names none, the part is counted: "A part this data does not carry". Its share stays in the recipe, because the shares of a recipe add up to 100.
- **What is being typed is the person's until it is sent.** An answer that comes while a number is half typed, or in the moment before a slider's change is sent, leaves it alone: it may be the answer to a sentence that was being read, and the digits typed next would be joined to its number and sent. Once a value is sent, the control shows it until the next answer, and then shows what the API returned.
- **A value is held against what is on its way.** A control sends nothing when the value it is set to is the one it already stands at. What it stands at is the value it last sent, while that waits for its answer, and the spec's after. So a slider moved to 70 and back to 50 before the answer sends both, and the API, which applies edits in order, ends at 50. A change of direction is sent with the weight the person last set for the same reason: a `set` always reads its `value`, and a later edit wins. A number typed after a step is always sent, because where a step lands is the API's to say.
- **A weight of 0 is off.** The API keeps an entry of 0 for a usual setting a person took off, so that a change of tenure does not bring it back (contract 5.1). The website draws it as a switch that is off, and as a chip that says "does not count" and has no "Remove". Switched on again it is asked for as anything is. `counts()` in `src/lib/search/counts.ts` is the one test for it. Before this the switch sprang back on when the answer came, and "ignore the high street" was shown as a wish for one.
- The kinds of home that go with each tenure are held in `src/content/settings.ts` (section 13, gap 8). A visit has none: the API refuses every kind for one.

### 5.2 Chips

Chips are drawn from the spec, never from the edits. A chip opens the same control the settings hold, in place, and has a "Remove" button where the setting can be removed.

**The chips wrap, and none is cut.** They once stood on one line that scrolled sideways with no sign that it did: on a desk the workplace read "Cindermoor", which is the name of an area, and on a phone it was off the screen with 21 px of "Turn" in sight. Now a chip that does not fit on a line goes onto the next, whole, and a name too long for a line takes a second inside its chip. "Turn" and the way out of a chip keep their width: what gives way is the words.

**What was asked for by way of character comes first.** The chips run vibes, then the things that count nearby, then the places to reach, the budget, any area hidden or kept, and renting or buying. They once ran tenure, budget, place and then the vibes, and three were drawn, so beside a budget and a workplace no vibe was ever seen. Six of what the search holds are drawn at first. Each says every part of itself, with the word "assumed" on each part that nobody said, as it does once the row is opened out: until 2026-09-27 it said what was said, and that Burro assumed the rest where any part of it was not (`IN_THE_ROW`, section 1). Where the search holds more than six, "+2 more" opens the row out and "Show fewer" closes it. Opened out, every chip is drawn, and what explains them is drawn under them. Opening a chip opens the row out for it.

**The usual settings have no chip.** Until 2026-09-26 a chip stood last in the row, "Usual settings: 6", marked "assumed", which opened the settings. The founder walked the website, and wrote: "Unsure what unusal settings:6 assumed refers to. either show those or remove the section." A usual setting that nobody chose moves no area ([ADR 0024](../adr/0024-an-area-with-no-figure-for-what-was-asked-stands-below.md)), so the chip told a person nothing they could use. It is gone from the row, and from what a screen reader hears of the row. Every setting is in sight, with its value, under "Refine search".

**A chip is drawn with its thing.** At one end the small drawing of what it is of, then its words, in the reading face because they hold figures, and at the other end a cross on sand, which takes it off. The drawing is chosen by the id and the family the service gives a vibe or a measure, and by its kind for the rest: a journey, a budget, renting, buying or visiting, an area. It is dress, and says nothing: what a chip is of is said by its words.

| State | Drawn | Said |
|---|---|---|
| A person said it | A solid edge of ink | As before |
| Nobody said it | A solid edge of ink, on cream, as what was said has. That it stands on sand is built too, and one line chooses (`ASSUMED_STANDS_ON`) | "assumed", as before. It is never told apart by its ground alone |
| It counts for nothing | Its thing in outline | "does not count", as before |
| It is in hand | On sand | |
| It is open | On amber, with the control it opens in a box under it, whose inner rule is amber | It says that it is expanded |

- **The heading of the row is a plate**: the words in the colour that is read on, on ink, in the face of names at 20 px. Nothing stands beside it. Until 2026-09-26 a line beside it said who had read the words, "Read by AI. Check what it understood." or "Read without AI." The founder asked for it to be taken away. Whether a language model reads what is typed was said before anything is typed, in the line under the box, which was what the draft of the privacy notice promised. That line went on the evening of 2026-09-26 too (section 1), and the drafts say what that changes. What Burro took on a guess says "assumed" on its chip.
- **On a narrow row two chips that say little share a row**, and one of them sets its words on two lines where it must. Every other chip has the row from edge to edge. Which two share is worked out from their words, counted in letters, by the width of the row and not of the screen. Left to wrap as they fell, the chips of a plain search took six rows of a phone, and took four, as they were counted with the chip of the usual settings among them.
- **On a narrow row the chips of a search of many things fold.** Up to four rows, every chip is in sight: a plain search was six chips in four rows, and is five chips since the usual settings have none. With more, the first two rows stand, and a button opens the rest and says how many it holds, "+4 more": two rows and the button were 150 px while it stood under them. **Since 2026-09-27 it is one button on a row of any width, and stands on the line of the heading, over the chips**, so that it is where it was once they are opened out. It keeps the focus, and folds the chips again. Where nothing is left to fold it goes, and hands the focus to the row. What is folded is one press away, and nothing of it is lost. After the third example, whose chips took five rows, the first result of a phone ended 27 px under the screen, and is whole. `NARROW_ROWS` in `ChipRow/rows.ts` holds the two figures.
- **On a wide row the chips of a search of many things fold too**, since the page came to ask nothing: Burro takes what it noticed in a sentence, so a sentence of a dozen things is a search of a dozen chips. At 1440 by 900, after the founder's own sentence, six chips stood in five rows, 280 px high, and the first result began at 909 of 900, out of sight. Up to three rows every chip is in sight, as it was: the five chips of a plain search stand in three, and its first result begins at 687. With more, the chips of the first two rows stand, and the line that opens the rest says how many it holds, which is three rows again: after the same sentence the first result begins at 744. A wide row lays its chips as they fall, so how they lie is read from the row once it is laid out, before the page is drawn, and the rows that fold are never seen unfolded. A browser that lays nothing out folds nothing. `WIDE_ROWS` in the same file holds the two figures.
- **A chip is drawn small on every screen**, at two pixels of the screen to one of its drawing. While the page asked, the chips under a question of a place were drawn so on a wide screen, so that the first result was in sight. Since the page was mended every chip of a wide screen is: it gave the answer 136 px (section 4.1).
- **On a narrow row a chip is 40 px high with its edge, and what is pressed in it 36**, where a main control is 44. It is among what the look gave up so that the first result is whole on the first screen of a phone: before it gave way, the first result of a plain search ended at 994 of 844. Section 9 says it. The row is a chip shorter since 2026-09-26, and a chip of a phone has its 44 px back: measured that day, a chip is 44 px high at 390 by 844 and 52 at 1440 by 900, and after a plain search the first result of a phone is whole with 6 px to spare (section 4.1).
- **On a desktop a chip is 48 px high with its edge**, and what is pressed in it 44, a main control. Drawn at the pixel of the screen, as every other drawing of a desk is, it was 66 px high and 72 with its shadow. Both are built, and one line chooses: `ON_A_WIDE_SCREEN` in `ChipRow/drawn.ts`.
- **A chip that has just been added drops into its place, in four steps, once.** Only the chip that is new. What comes of a first sentence arrives together. A search that is drawn a second time, as when a person comes back from the page of an area, brings nothing down, and nor does opening the row or a chip. To know which chip is new the row keeps the keys of the chips it last drew, and which searches it has drawn, in memory: no word, and nothing that was typed. Where the system asks for less movement nothing drops.

| Chip | Drawn for | Parts | Marked "assumed" when |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tenure | Always | "Renting", "Buying" or "Visiting" | `tenure_from` is `default` or `inferred`, and the person has not picked it before anything was asked for. Where the page took of an offer that a person rents or buys, and the words gave another way |
| Budget | `budget.amount` is not `null`. A visit has none, and no chip of a budget or of a home | Amount, segment, "firm limit" or "flexible" | The part's code is in `assumed`: `segment`, `strictness`. From route 1's assumptions for a budget read from words, and every part that no edit sent with the amount states, for a budget that was set by its amount alone, as one chosen from an offer is. What one press sends is read together: the kind of home it sends in an edit of its own is said, and is not marked |
| Place | Each of `commutes` | Name, how, "N minutes", firm or flexible. It said "within N minutes" until the words were written again: "within" is a word Burro itself reads as a firm limit, and it stood beside "flexible" | `mode`, `max_minutes`, `strictness`: from route 1's assumptions for a place read from words, and every part the edit did not state for a place added with a control. `place`, where the name that was typed is borne by several places and the page took the first the service gave |
| Feature | Each of `weights` whose `provenance` is not `default` | `Metric.short_label`, and "more" or "fewer" where either. "does not count" where the weight is 0 | `provenance` is `inferred`, or `weight`, `direction` |
| Vibe | Each of `tags` | `Tag.label`. For a scale, the end that is asked for: "Going out: towards Calm", and "Turn", which asks for the other end with the weight it had. Where the recipe of the vibe holds recorded crime, "counts recorded crime", in the row as well as in full. Where the reader took a word one way of two, the word, quoted: "read from “gritty”" | `provenance` is `inferred`, or `weight`, `word` |
| Area | Each of `areas` | Name, "hidden" or "only" | The page took the rule of an offer, which it takes on the service's guess and on nothing else (`rule`). Never where a person set it, by its word in a plain list or by the button of a result |
| Which journey counts | The spec has two `commutes` or more. It comes after the places | That only the journey that does least well against its limit counts, or that the average of the journeys counts, each in a sentence. It opens the three settings of how journeys count | `commute_combine_from` is `default` or `inferred` |
| Usual settings | None is drawn, since 2026-09-26 | | |

An `Assumption` points at `operations[group][index]`. The website finds the chip by that edit's `place_id`, `feature_id`, `tag_id` or group, and adds the `code` to `assumed` for it. **What the page took of an offer is marked by what it took** (`Mark` in `src/lib/search/takes.ts`): whether a limit is firm, where the words gave another way than the one that was taken; how much a thing counts, where the way was Burro's guess or the gentler of two; which way, of a thing that runs two ways; the place, where it was the first of several; and a rule for an area, which is taken on the service's guess alone. What one press could take as it was plainly said, and was taken so, is not marked. The mark is the word "assumed". It goes when the person edits that part. Until 2026-09-26 a dashed edge said it a second time, for the eye: the founder walked the website, and wrote "The dashed border is not understood to a user, please make solid". No edge of the website is dashed since.

**Everything that explains the chips is drawn where it can be seen.** It was written, and then kept for a screen reader alone. The heading is small and visible, and says what the chips are: "Your search so far" while the spec is one of the two defaults the API served, "What Burro understood" once words have been read into a search, and "What is in this search" for a search made with the settings or opened from a link. Under the chips, once the row is opened out: what "assumed" means, wherever a chip is marked so. The same goes for the keys that move the map (section 6), and for the scale of a slider, which runs from 0 to 100 (section 5.1).

**What the person said is not marked as assumed.** "Renting assumed" stood on the chip after the person typed "Renting". The API now sets `tenure_from` whenever a tenure is said, whether or not it moves (section 13, gap 20, met), and the chip is drawn from that. One case is left to the website: before anything is asked for, "Rent or buy" sends nothing and swaps the defaults the API served, so no answer says the tenure was chosen. The store keeps that it was picked (`tenurePicked`), and the chip is then not marked. A place picked from the field was the other way about: it said "Public transport", as if that had been chosen, where the same place read from a sentence said "Public transport assumed". A place added with a control is marked in every part its edit did not state. And "£400,000, A flat assumed, firm limit" stood under the founder's sentence, which said "a 1 bed flat": one press sends the amount and the kind of home in an edit each, as they were offered, and the website read the edit that holds the amount alone. What is sent together is now read together (`withBudgetSet`). The mark was never about what Burro holds no figure for: that is said in the note of the offer.

**A word with two meanings is quoted.** Where a release holds no recorded crime and carries no Gritty, the reader takes "gritty" to mean Works and warehouses and says so (`Assumption.word`). The chip reads "Works and warehouses, read from “gritty” assumed", in the row as well as in full, until the person sets the vibe. The word is the reader's own, from its answer, and is never what was typed. Where the release carries Gritty, as the committed release and a release of London do, the chip reads "Gritty: towards Gritty, counts recorded crime", and nothing is assumed.

**No page tells a visitor that a vibe is a rough guide.** The founder decided on 2026-09-25 that Village feel is served though it did not reach the bar, and that it says that it is less sure than the other vibes (ADR 0013, as amended that day). So its label, "Rough guide", and the sentence that says why stood in sight wherever the vibe was shown: on its chip and under the row, on a result, beside its band on the page of an area, on the page of vibes, on the shelf and its card, beside its slider, in a comparison, and where the map is coloured by it. The founder walked the website a second time, and wrote: "remove the concept of rough guide, we don't want to pass this on to a user". Since the evening of 2026-09-26 no page draws the label or the sentence, and the key to the drawings has no row for it.

| Matter | What is so |
|---|---|
| What the service says | As it did. Which vibe is a rough guide is `sureness` of the vibe, and the label and the sentence are `rough_guides` of route 11. The website reads neither to draw anything |
| What a search does with such a vibe | What it does with any vibe. Where the reader offers it, the page takes the offer as it takes any, and its chip says "assumed" where a person did not say it in so many words (section 3). Until that evening no way of its offer was marked as Burro's guess, the button that added several things never took it, and it was never taken without a press of its own |
| Where it is still left out | It is in no line of what an area is like in short, and in no list of what two areas share: each is worked out from the vibes, and a rough guide is used to work out nothing else. It is among what an area has more of than most for no area, so "Search for these vibes" never adds it. None of it says anything to a visitor. What is the website's of it is one line, `LESS_SURE` in `src/content/rough.ts`, which may hold such a vibe back from nothing |
| What it gives up | A person reads where an area sits on Village feel as they read any band, and the recipe behind it reached the founder's bar on no try. [ADR 0013](../adr/0013-vibes-are-the-centre.md), as amended on 2026-09-26, has what that risks, and the review desk still says which vibe is one |

**A vibe that counts recorded crime says so wherever it is in a search.** "Somewhere a bit gritty" was read and ranked on recorded criminal damage among other things, and the words "recorded" and "crime" were nowhere on the page of results, even with the source of the sentence open. The chip of such a vibe now says "counts recorded crime" in the row. Its card on the shelf says "This vibe counts recorded crime", with what it counts and when recorded crime counts, above the buttons that add it: to add Gritty is to ask for recorded crime by name, so it is said before anything can be pressed. The control it opens, and its slider in the settings, say what it counts: "This vibe counts recorded crime: Recorded criminal damage; Recorded anti-social behaviour." So does the source of every sentence about it on a result, after the sources. Which vibe holds recorded crime, and which parts of its recipe are of crime, is read from route 11 (`countsOf` in `src/content/crime.ts`).

**"The slowest journey" is not what `slowest` means.** It is the journey worth least against its own limit, which need not be the one that takes the most minutes (contract 6.4). The settings, the chip, the card and Methods all say "the journey that does least well against its limit": it was "does worst" until the words were written again.

### 5.3 How typing and controls stay in step

One store holds the search. It is a React context over a plain object, with no library. `state.ts` holds the object and the one pure function that moves it on. `store.ts` holds it in memory and hands it to the page. `flow.ts` holds what a person can do, as the calls each makes. The session keeps the store while the tab is open (section 2), so a page that is not the search page reads the search with `useOpenSearch`. Beside the fields below, the store holds `pending` (edits not yet answered), `refused` (edits a control sent that the reducer rejected), `answers` (a count of the answers that leave no edit waiting, against which a control holds what it was set to), `untouched` (true until an answer changes the spec), `kept` (the search as it was when the sentence being read was sent, for Stop to put back), `settingsOpen` and `settingsByPage` (true while the settings are open because the page opened them and the person has not used them), `tenurePicked`, `quoted` (the word a vibe was read from, by chip, where the reader took a word one way of two), `budgetWent`, `rankedBefore` (how many areas the ranking before this one ranked), `box` (what the search knows of the box, in counts: how far into it the search has read and whether words before that were changed since, where the words that were last read begin, and how far it will have read once the words that are being read are answered, and had read when they were sent, for "Stop" to put back, how many characters that were read have been taken out of the box, and what the search knew of the box at the times the keys that undo and that do again lead back to, no more than 64 of each), and what could not be loaded, each with the failure that says why.

`answers` does not move when a reading comes while an edit waits. The edit has not been answered, so the control that was moved keeps showing what it was set to until the ranking comes. A control nobody has touched is drawn from the spec whenever the spec changes.

| Field | Holds | Written from |
|---|---|---|
| `phase`, `failure`, `seq` | The phase, the last failure, the request counter. A failure is the API's own (`api`, with its `code`, `message`, `fields` and `X-Request-Id`) or the client's: `timeout`, `network`, `offline`, `aborted`, `not_configured` (no address is set for the API) or `unreadable` (an answer came back that is not the API's envelope) | The flow |
| `spec`, `specHash` | `PreferenceSpec`, read-only | Only `InterpretData.spec`, `RankData.spec`, `ShareData.spec` and `Defaults` |
| `read` | `operations`, `assumptions`, `clarify`, `unmet`, `rejected`, `notice`, `notice_text`, `interpreter`, `degraded`, `model_refused`, `suggestions` and `unread`. A suggestion holds where its words stand in the text, and `unread` where the stretches that were not read stand: as offsets, and never as words. Both go when the box changes. `partUnread` says that a stretch was not read, and stays. Since the page asks nothing a suggestion is held no longer than it takes to take it. With them, what the page made of what was noticed: `took`, how many offers it took; `things`, what each was of, so that what the rules took of a thing a model's reading does not take again; and `left`, what it took nothing of, each with why and with the service's own words for it. None holds anything that was typed | Route 1, and `takes.ts` |
| `ranking`, `rankedHash`, `rankedBy` | `scores`, `ranked`, `filtered`, `unranked`, `empty_spec`, `areas_ranked`, `areas_listed`. The hash of the spec that was ranked. The release and the engine that ranked it | Route 2 or 10, and the `meta` of its answer |
| `explanations`, `explainedHash`, `explainedBy` | `Explanation[]`. The hash of the spec they are for. The release and the engine that wrote them | Route 3, its `spec_hash`, and the `meta` of its answer |
| `facts`, `factsBy`, `details`, `detailsBy` | `Fact` by `fact_id`, `AreaData` by `area_id`, and the release and the engine that made each | Routes 3 and 6, and the `meta` of each answer |
| `explainFailure`, `detailFailures` | Why the reasons of a search, or the profile of an area, could not be loaded: the failure whole, as `failure` holds one | Routes 3 and 6 |
| `placeNames` | A name by `place_id` | `places` of the answer that brought the spec: routes 1, 2 and 10. A place an answer gave no name for, which the contract does not allow, is "A place with no name in this data" |
| `assumed` | `AssumptionCode`s by chip | Route 1 |
| `shared` | The id of the share the search came from, with its `coarsened`, `stale` and `original_release_id`. `null` for a search the person began | Route 10 |
| `selectedId`, `hoveredId` | Area ids | List, map, table |

| The person | The website sends | Then |
|---|---|---|
| Sends a sentence | Route 1 with `spec` and, as `text`, what the box holds after the words the search has read. Before anything is read that is all it holds | If any edit changed the spec, routes 2 (`limit` 20) and 3 (`limit` 5) together, then route 6 for the first five. Once the answer is in, and an edit, an offer or a question came of the words, the search has read all that the box held when they were sent |
| Moves a control or edits a chip | Route 2 with `spec`, the one edit and `limit` 20 | If `spec_hash` changed, route 3. If the edit is in `rejected`, the control goes back to the returned spec and says why |
| Adds a word of the shelf | Route 2 with the spec and one `TagEdit` | As a control. The shelf goes, and the focus goes to what Burro understood |
| Sends a sentence that is no plain list | Route 1, as above. Then route 2 with the spec route 1 returned and the `operations` of every way the page took, as the API gave them, in one request, in the order the things were noticed, with the edit of each journey whose place was the first of several. Nothing is sent of what was left | As a control. Where a model reads as well, what it noticed beyond the rules is sent in a second request when it comes, with whatever waits |
| Chooses what Deep search is to hold, before a search | Route 2 with `spec`, the one edit and `limit` 1. The ranking is thrown away, and no reasons are asked for: the edit is sent so that the settings are drawn from the spec the API returned. To choose renting, buying or visiting first sends nothing | Nothing is ranked for the page until "Search with these requirements" is pressed |
| Looks at the map by a vibe, before a search | Nothing. The bands of every vibe came with the page | Nobody is told which vibe a person looks at |
| Presses "Search for these vibes" on an area's page | Nothing at once. The session keeps the edits (`wanted`), and the search page, when it is drawn, sends route 2 with the spec and one `TagEdit` for each vibe | As a word of the shelf. The edits are taken once. The request names no area |
| Opens a vibe, or a part of an area's page that is closed | Nothing. It came with the page | |
| Opens a shared link | Route 10 with the id from the fragment | Routes 3 (`limit` 5) and 6 for the first five. After that the search is refined like any other |
| Makes a link | Route 9 with `spec` and `exact_destinations` | Nothing. The search is as it was. The link is kept by the session, beside the search and not in it, so that it is still there when the person comes back from another page |
| Opens a comparison | Route 7 with `area_ids` and `spec` | Nothing. The search is as it was |

The website waits 8 s for route 1, 5 s for routes 2, 3, 7, 9 and 10, 3 s for route 8 and 10 s for a `GET`. A first answer is shown no sooner than the wait of section 1, which holds back what is shown and no request: a failure is said at once. Both paths end in a spec the reducer made, so they cannot drift. After the first edit the API returns the default weights at a quarter, rounded down to a step (contract 5.3, rule 11): the controls are redrawn from the returned spec and the live region says "Settings you did not choose now count for less."

## 6. The map

**The map is in sight whichever way a search begins, and is larger once one is open.** The founder walked the website, and wrote: "The map should stay visible on both tabs", and of the page of results, "Map should be larger on the search result".

| Matter | What is so |
|---|---|
| Before a search, on a wide screen | From 60rem it stands beside the two tabs, in the second of the two columns, and does not move or change size as the tab changes |
| Before a search, on a screen of one column | It stands over the two ways in, so that it is in sight whichever is chosen, as a low strip with "Show the whole map" over it: 120 px high, or a fifth of the width of the window where that is more, and 88 px where the window is under 44rem high. It shows the city as wide as the page, and names no area. Its controls, its key and the table of all areas come with the whole map. As the page was first rebuilt the map stood under the tabs there, and on a phone began at 885 of 844 |
| After a search, on a wide screen | From 60rem it stands beside the answer, and stays in sight as the answer scrolls, in a box that scrolls in itself. It is wide enough to name areas. `--columns` in the tokens says how wide the answer and the map are, as shares of the page: seven to six, which at 1440 gives the box of the map 543 px, where it had 272 |
| After a search, on a narrow screen | It is as wide as the page, and stands after the first result |
| What it is coloured by | By a vibe before a search, while the card of a word is open on the shelf of Quick search. While Deep search is chosen that card is not drawn, and the map is coloured by no vibe. By the fit once there is a ranking |

**Measured on 2026-09-26, of the website as it stands.** At 1440 by 900 the box of the map is 543 px wide, beside an answer of 633, and the window of the map in it is 519 by 386. Before a search the box is 608 px high and the map names ten areas. After a plain search that ranks five it names nine, three of them under their pins. At 390 by 844 the strip is 374 px wide: before a search its box is 198 px high, its window 118, and it names no area; after a search it stands after the first result, its window is 296 px high, and it names three areas, under their pins. The whole map of a phone has a window of 358 by 386, and names ten areas after one press of "Zoom in". What else this section gives of how wide the map is was measured while it stood in a column of 17rem, and says so.

`src/lib/map/style.ts` is the only file that holds a colour, a layer or a source of the map. A basemap is added there and nowhere else. MapLibre draws no text, because a label needs glyph files from a host: every name is in the DOM, the names on the map among them.

| Layer, from the bottom | Drawn from | Look |
|---|---|---|
| Background | Nothing | `--map-water`, with the grain of water on it: the four glints of a tile, in `--map-glint`. The river and the sea are what is left between the areas: the API serves no water |
| Fill | Route 5, with `area_id` as the feature's id | Empty state: `--map-land`. Before a search, while the card of a word of the shelf is open: the five bands of that vibe, from route 4's `bands`. With results: five bands of `Score.score` in steps of 20, `--map-1` to `--map-5` |
| Grain | A picture made in code from the drawing of a tile of meadow | Three tufts to a tile, on every area that has a band, in the colour of the band one deeper than its own. It is 1.35 to 2 against its ground. It is the same on every band and says nothing: a grain is a grain. None is laid under lines or dots |
| Pattern | Pictures made in code when the map starts | Lines that run up to the right on each `filtered` area, dots on each `unranked` one, and dots on each area a vibe cannot place, each drawn in pixels in the colour of the border |
| Outline | Route 5 | 1 px `--map-line`, which is 3 to 1 or more on the water, on land with no fit and on each of the five bands. 2 px on hover or focus, 3 px on the selected area |
| Pins | DOM buttons at `AreaSummary.centroid` | The first ten ranked. The drawing of a pin, 26 px by 32 on every screen: a head of cream with a point under it, which stands on its place by its point, and the rank set on its head in type, in the reading face. Two pins are kept 28 px apart. Its name reads "Rank 1, Alderwick, fit 78 of 100", and then "Based on 4 of the 9 things that count in your search" where the fit rests on part of what counts |
| Names | DOM, tied to `AreaSummary.centroid` | A name is drawn whole, in one line or two, or not at all. An area of the first ten is named under its pin wherever the window of the map has room for the name, the better ranked first. Any other area is named over itself, where its own area has room for the whole of its name. A name is never cut, by its area or by the edge of the window, and never lies over a pin or over another name: near an edge it stands against the edge, as far to one side as leaves the whole of its pin over it. While the map is moved a name that comes to the edge is not drawn. It is worked out again when the map is drawn nearer or further, and once it has been moved. Every word laid over the map is set in a face of the website, and none in the face the map library names. It takes no press: the pointer goes through it to the area. It is kept from a screen reader, which has every name in the table and on the pins. It is set in the face of names at 20 px, on a plate of cream with an edge of ink, so that it reads the same on every band and over lines and dots, and nothing is read on the land. Measured on 2026-09-26 on the made-up city of 24 areas, of the website as it stands: at 1440 by 900, in a window of the map 519 px wide, ten areas are named before a search, and nine after a plain search that ranks five, three of the five under their pins. On a phone none is named before a search, three are on the strip after one, under their pins, and ten on the whole map after one press of "Zoom in". While the map stood in a column of 17rem, that morning, none was named before a search at either size: at 1440 by 900 the map was 242 px wide inside its box, and no area had room for its name. Before the look a name was 12 px high, and 12 of 24 were named before a search in a map of 440 px |
| Card | DOM, on select | Name, borough, rank, fit and how much of what counts the fit rests on, or the reason it has no rank. The name is a link to the page of the area, before a search and after one: an area pressed on the map once led nowhere. It is drawn as a result draws the name of its area, in the face of names with a hard line under it, and so is a name in the table of every area. Every way on of the card is a button of the look. "Show in the list", "Compare", which puts the area in the tray, and "Close" |
| Controls | DOM, in a row under the map | Zoom in, zoom out, whole city: buttons of the look, 44 px high, the first two with a plus and a minus drawn in pixels, with the keys that move the map written beside them, except on a screen that is touched and has nothing to point with, which has no keys. The legend. "Table of all areas", which opens the table in place. Nothing but a pin is laid over the map: a button there covers an area |

| Shown | By colour | And always by |
|---|---|---|
| Rank | | The number in the pin, and the order of the list and the table |
| Score | The band | The band's range in the legend, in figures. "Fit 78 of 100" in the pin's name, the map card and the table |
| Filtered, not ranked | | The pattern, and the reason in words in the map card and the table |
| Selected | | The 3 px outline, a pin whose head is amber, of the size it was, and `aria-current` on the card and the table row. The card of the pin has its inner rule in amber. Nothing changes size under a press |

- **The lens.** Before a search the map can be coloured by one vibe: the one whose card is open on the shelf, where the API says it may colour a map (`Tag.lens`). On a narrow screen the map is below the form, and a word that was pressed once coloured a map two screens down, with no legend. So there the card of the word holds the city itself, drawn in the same five bands with what each band is, and the card opens by its word, where the whole of it has room, or under it with its head in sight (section 3). In the card the way to add the word comes before what the vibe is made of, so that it is in sight too. The picture is `VibeMap`, which the page of vibes draws as well, from the boundaries and the bands that came with the page. The legend names the vibe and both its ends. Until 2026-09-27 it was a list of five bands, "Band 1, Calm" to "Band 5, Buzzy", or "least" and "most" for a vibe that runs one way. It is now the gauge of the vibe: the small drawing of the vibe with its name, and under it the five shades of the map in a row, from the palest to the darkest, between the one picture of each end of the vibe, with the name of the end under its picture. The gauge is one picture to whoever hears the page, and its name says which end is the palest and which the darkest. An area the vibe cannot place is drawn with dots and said to be so, and is never put in the middle. The table gains a column for the vibe, and the card of the map says the band in words, a mixed area as a range. No area is pinned, because none is ranked. Nothing is sent: the bands of every vibe come with the page in one answer, so nobody is told which vibe a person looks at. A ranking colours the map by fit again.
- **On a narrow screen the map is a strip.** Once a search is open it is 300 px high and stands after the first result, so that the answer comes first. It was 240 px until the page was mended, and 176 while it stood above the list, where pins lay over one another in it. A pin is 26 px by 32 there, as on every screen. "Show the whole map" stands over the strip. It makes the map as high as the window is wide, and no higher than 70% of the window, and brings its controls and its legend, and pressed again it takes them away. It keeps its name and says whether it is on (`aria-pressed`), and is amber while it is: until 2026-09-27 its name turned to "Show less of the map" as well, so that a screen reader said "Show less of the map, pressed", and the button was 5 px wider under the press that turned it. Once a search is open the table is one press from the map at either height. Before a search, on one column, it comes with the whole map, and is two presses away. In a strip a finger scrolls the page and moves no map.
- **In step.** `selectedId` and `hoveredId` are in the store. Hovering or focusing a card sets the outline on the map. Pressing a pin or an area opens the card of the map, by the map. The card takes the focus, the pin says that it is expanded, Escape closes the card, and the page is not moved: on a desk, where the list and the map stand side by side, the result was marked at the press and the page went 641 px to it, from under the hand. The list is told when "Show in the list" is pressed, and brings the result into sight then. So an area that is chosen on the map is not marked in the list until that button is pressed. **"Show on the map", under the working of a result, leads the other way, and says what it did**: it marks the area on the map, the card of the map takes the focus and is brought into sight, and a line under the button says that the area is now marked there, which is heard as it is written. It says so for as long as that area is the one chosen. Measured on 2026-09-27: on a phone the card then stands from 375 to 668 of 844, where the focus was left on the button, 1,492 px under the window. **"Show" in a row of the table leads to the map as well, and says what it did**, since 2026-09-27. Its name says where it leads: "Show Marrowfen on the map and in the list". The area is marked on the map, its card opens under the map and takes the focus, and the map brings itself into sight with the card under it, by as little as must be. Beside the answer the column of the map is scrolled to the map, and the list is told, as it is not of a pin: it brings the result of the area into sight, since the card stays in the window as the page goes there. On a narrow screen the page goes to the map, and "Show in the list" on the card leads on to the result. Over the table a line says that the area is now marked on the map, which is heard as it is written, for as long as that area is the one chosen. So what was pressed goes from under the hand, once, and a press that a browser counts as the second of two lands on nothing of the map. Where the map and the card are both in sight already, nothing is moved. Until then the row was held where it stood under the press, while the card opened over the head of the window, out of sight: the button said that it was pressed, and nothing was heard. Measured that day, after "Show" in the eighth row of the table, of a search for two vibes and a journey: at 1440 by 900 the window of the map stands from 25 to 382 of 900 and the card from 485 to 764, and on a phone the map from 78 to 374 of 844 and the card from 388 to 681. Nothing pans unless the selected area is off screen.
- **"Show in the list" opens the list at the top of the card.** A card is taller than a phone's screen. Brought to the nearest edge, it opened at its middle, 1,056 px past its heading. It is brought in by its top, and then given the focus.
- **The map can be skipped to, and skipped.** "Skip to the map" stands beside "Skip to results", before the box. The map comes before the results in the page, so "Skip the map" is the first thing in it, and leads past its pins and its controls. What it leads to says "End of the map" to whoever hears the page, and draws no word: the focus landed on a thing with no name, and a screen reader had nothing to say of it. "Skip to results" is there once there are results: before a search it led to nothing.
- **The equivalent list.** `AreaTable` holds every area of the release: rank, name linked to its page, borough, fit, and status in words, in rank order and then by name. It is one press from the map, closed at first so that the answer comes first, and says everything the map does. Beside a fit that rests on part of what counts it says how much, for every area the ranking ranks. An area with no rank says "Not ranked" and "No fit": it said "None", which reads as something the program left behind. The box the table scrolls in is positioned, so that the name kept for a screen reader in its last column is cut off with the rest: laid out against the page, it made the page 31 px wider than a phone and the whole page scrolled sideways.
- **Without WebGL** the map panel shows one line saying why, and the way to the table.
- **Tests.** `fillFor(scores, filtered, unranked)`, `fillForVibe(marks)` and `buildStyle(theme)` are pure and tested. `maplibre-gl` is replaced in Jest by a stand-in that records calls. `buildStyle` is also held to MapLibre's own style specification, because no browser can say whether the style is valid. The map's style sheet is held against the library's, by the weight of their selectors: the library puts its own class on the map's element and sets `position: relative` on it, and where two rules weigh the same the one loaded last wins, which is the library's. With one class the map's element was no longer laid over its frame, had no height, and drew nothing. `test/support/css.ts` reads a style sheet as far as that needs.
- **Loaded on demand.** `src/lib/map/library.ts` imports the map library and its style sheet. `MapView` loads that file only once it knows the browser has WebGL and the boundaries are in hand, so a browser that cannot draw the map never fetches it.
- **Colours are read from the page.** `themeOfPage` reads the tokens of section 8 from the map's own element, once, when the map starts. The look is one, so the map does not ask the system whether it prefers dark. `style.ts` names the tokens, and holds the colours of the look to fall back on where no token can be read: a test holds them to `tokens.css`.
- **The patterns and the grain are worked out, not fetched.** Each is a tile of pixels made in code, so no image file is asked for. The grain must be in another colour on each band, so the map cannot take the picture of a tile as it is: `style.ts` holds the two grains point for point, and a test reads the drawing of the tiles and fails where they differ.
- **A pattern is sharp however near the map is drawn.** The map library draws a pattern larger the nearer the map is, by anything from once to twice, and smooths it. So each picture is handed over with 8 pixels to one of its drawing, and the library is told how many make one of the screen, again after every zoom. A pixel of the grain is 2 px of the screen at every zoom.
- **The box of the map stands whole beside the answer.** The map and all that is said of it stand in one box of the look: the map, what moves it, the keys, the card of a pin, the legend, the way to the whole map and to the table. Measured while it stood in a column of 17rem: beside the answer, in a window 900 px high, the box had 868 px. It held 906, scrolled in itself, and its rule and its shadow were cut off at its foot. In a column as narrow as a phone the window of the map was made 15rem high at the least, where it is 18rem in a wider one, and the box held 858. What it gave up was water. **In the wider column it has since 2026-09-26**, measured that day at 1440 by 900: the box is 725 px high after a search that leaves no area out, and 757 where the legend says that areas were left out, and it scrolls in nothing but the page. As the page opens it stands under the name board, from 165, so it is whole in a window 900 high in the first case, with 10 px under it, and in the second its foot was 22 px under the window until the page had gone as far. **Since 2026-09-27 the window of the map is as high as the screen has left for it**, and never higher than six parts in ten of the screen: measured that day at 1440 by 900, with areas left out, it is 360 px high where it was 389, the box is 727.5, and its foot stands 7.5 px over the foot of the screen as the page opens. In a window 1,000 high nothing changed. On a screen lower than about 830 px the window of the map is at its least height, 18rem, and the box still ends under the screen until the page is scrolled: by 20.5 px at 1280 by 800, where it was by 121.6, by 100.5 at 1280 by 720 and by 97.5 at 1024 by 768. Once the answer is scrolled it stands from 16, whole.
- **The water does not move, and nothing is drawn that the data does not hold.** No shore, hill, tree, boat or building, no compass and no name board over the map.
- **Where an area is** is two small pictures on glinting water, each within an edge of ink. The city is one shape in sand, and the place is marked by one whole square and a spot: no drawing draws a line in dots or in dashes. The area drawn close is amber: it was the deepest green, which on the map means the best fit.
- **Nothing to rank by.** When `empty_spec` is true every area scores 0. The map then gives no area a band and no pin, and the list and the table show no fit.

## 7. Component inventory

Parts: A is data and shell, B is search, C is map and access. A prop type is a generated schema. `onEdit` takes an `Operations`. The kit of the look, the town of an area and what the look added to a part that was there stand in three tables of their own, after this one.

| Component | Part | Runs | Props | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SyntheticBanner` | A | Server | `synthetic: Meta["synthetic"]` | Every page |
| `PreviewBanner` | A | Server | `preview: Meta["preview"]` | Every page |
| `LivePreviewBanner` | A | Browser | `preview: Meta["preview"]`, of the release the page was built on. It shows the banner for that, and as soon as any answer says its release is a preview | The shell |
| `AsItWasBuilt` | A | Browser | `meta: Meta`, of the release the page was built on, and the page. It shows the page while every answer is of the same kind of data, and a notice in its place once one is not | The shell, round every page |
| `LiveSyntheticBanner`, `RemembersSynthetic` | A | Browser | None. The first shows the banner as soon as any answer says the data is made up. The second tells the tab that the page it is on was built on made-up data, and draws nothing | The shell. The page for a fault in the layout |
| `SiteHeader`, `SiteFooter`, `SkipLink` | A | Server | None. `meta: Meta`, for the release and the engine. `href`, which is `#main` unless given | Every page, inside `Shell`, which takes `meta: Meta` |
| `Sentence` | A | Server | `sentence: ExplainedSentence`, `facts: Fact[]`, and `source`, which is false where the key of its source stands elsewhere, as it does for the trade-off of a result. Left out, the sentence ends in its key | Card |
| `FactRow` | A | Server | `fact: Fact`, and `href` where the name of the row leads to a page | Area, card, compare |
| `SourceNote` | A | Browser | `facts: Fact[]` | Wherever a figure shows |
| `CostRange` | A | Server | `fact: Fact`, `estimate: CostEstimate`, `budget?: Budget`, `scale?`: what every bar of a list is drawn along, from `scaleOf` | Card |
| `SourceLine` | A | Server | `facts: Fact[]` | Area, through `FactRow` with `source="line"` |
| `LocatorMap` | A | Server, as SVG | `outlines: Outline[]` from `outlinesOf(geometry)`, `areaId`, `name`. The outlines are worked out once for a release, because every picture draws every area | Area, card |
| `AreaProfile` | A | Server | `data: AreaData`, `meta: MetaData`, `geometry: GeometryData`, `areas`, the id, slug and name of every area, for the links to the areas most like this one, and `bands`, the band of every area on every vibe, for what two areas share. A station is a `station` fact in a `FactRow`: there is no `StationList` | Area |
| `Portrait` | A | Server | `data: AreaData`, `meta`, the vibes and the features of the release, `besideShort`, what stands beside what the area is like in short, and `underMore`, what stands under the vibes the area has more of than most. Each vibe the area is placed on is a native `details` | Area |

| `PromptBox`, `Examples` | B | Browser | `maxText: ServedLimits["max_text"]`, `busy`, `onSubmit(text)`, `open` (a search is open), `onStartAgain`, `onTyped` (told that the box changed, never what to), and as children what Burro says of the search, drawn directly under the box. `onSubmit` is handed what the box holds after the words the search has read. Its handle reads those words, fills the box with an example, and selects a part of them, counted from where they begin. It asks the store how far the search has read and tells it where the box was changed, in counts (`useMarks`), or is handed both as `marks`. Of what came into the box it says that the keys that undo put it back, or the keys that do again, where they did, with where what they left selected begins, and that it was pasted from among what was read, where it was. It keeps where what was last copied from it began, how many characters it was and whether it has been read, to know the last by. `Examples` takes `onUse(example)` | Search |

The bar takes none: on every page it takes no room until an area is chosen, and then stays at the foot of the screen, where it has four places: the town and the name of each area that is chosen, with the cross that takes it out, a place kept empty for each that is left, and the way to the comparison. Its heading is drawn once an area is chosen and not before: until then the bar is named without one, since a heading with nothing under it led a person who goes by headings to nothing, and on the first page was the only heading under the first. A bar takes `drawn`, the drawing that stands before its name, `holds`, what it holds in a few words, which describes the button and is no part of its name, `bring` and `rest`. One of a stack has a rule of ink between it and the next, one that stands alone has an edge of ink and the shadow of the look, and one on the grass is a box of the kit. There are two since 2026-09-26, in the panel of Quick search | Search |

Where it stands at the end of a line that is higher than it, the whole of its place takes the press | Search |

One line over what Burro understood, which names each thing and opens to why, in the browser's own `details`. It is a note, and a band of amber marks it as one. Since 2026-09-27 it stands in no box of its own, since the box of the search is one already: as a box of one line it ran to two and three lines on a phone, 60 px and 88, and the first result ended under the first screen. | `Fold`, `OpenToAddress` | A | Server. Browser | `says`, what a person would ask, `of`, what it is asked of, for whoever hears the page, `id`, and `kind`: a bar inside a box, a small one on a rule, or one that stands on the grass.
