| `StepButton` | B | Browser | `way`, `name`, `off`, `onPress`. The minus or the plus of a step, as a drawing | Settings |

**Three parts of the kit went on 2026-09-27, which no page drew**: `Menu`, the choices of a question with the carrot beside the one in hand, which was drawn where Burro asked and by nothing since he asks nothing; `Sheet`, the panel a thing opens to, which no page ever used; and `Step`, a figure between a minus and a plus, which the settings draw with `StepButton` and a field of their own. The carrot is still drawn beside the choice in hand of a list, by the box of the search and by the field of a place, each in its own sheet.

**The kit of the look**, in `src/components/kit/`. A part of it is native HTML under a drawing: what is pressed is a `button` or an `a`, what is chosen is a `fieldset` of buttons, and what is opened is a button that says whether it is open. A drawing is dress: it says nothing of its own, and what it stands for is said in words. A page takes the look from the kit and from the tokens, and builds no frame, button or drawing of its own.

| Part of the kit | Props | What it is |
|---|---|---|
| `Frame` | `kind`, which is `box`, `box-on` or `plain`, `as`, `bare` | The box of the look: cream, a double rule of ink with a notch at each corner and a hard shadow, as a picture cut in nine over a plain edge of ink. `box-on` is the box of what is in hand, with its inner rule in amber. `plain` is a plain edge and no shadow. Where it stands on the grass, and in no other frame, it keeps two art pixels clear at its far side, as a box keeps the room of its shadow there: the edge of ink of every frame of a column ends on one line. `bare` leaves the room inside to the page |
| `Press` | `kind`, which is `go`, `plain` or `stop`, `on`, `off`, `onPress` or `href`, `expanded`, `controls` | A button or a link, 44 px high or more. `go` is cobalt, for the one button that matters most in sight. `plain` is cream. `stop` is cream within an edge of poppy. `on` is amber. Under a press its picture gives way to the picture of the button pressed, which its face asks for with the page, as a ground of no size: asked for at the first press, it had not come, and the button was drawn with no edge |
| `Label` | `thing`, `says`, `state`, `onOpen`, `open`, `onTakeOff`, `arrives` | A chip: the drawing of a thing, its words, and a cross that takes it off |
| `Thing` | `kind`, `id`, `family`, `state` | The small drawing of a vibe, a measure, a journey, a budget, renting or buying, an area or the usual settings. The one named for the id, then the one for the family, then the plain one. A group of measures that is of no family of vibes is drawn as a family is, by the dimension the service gives its measures, which is handed on as `family`. Solid, or in outline for what counts for nothing |
| `Peg`, `Ends` | `band`, `low`, `high`, `spread`, `asked`, `part`, `pictured`. `Ends` takes `id` too, the id the service gives the vibe | Where an area sits on a vibe: five steps and a peg, and one small picture at each end of the gauge. With the id of its vibe each end has the picture that is drawn for that end of that vibe, whichever way the vibe runs (`picturesAtEnds`), and the blank one at both ends where the vibe has none. Without it an end is drawn by its name, as it was. `kit/Ends/look.ts` chooses which way an end is drawn, where one was drawn two ways. Never a bar that fills |
| `Gauge` | `counts`, `steps`, `ends` | How much a thing counts: a row of steps that fill |
| `Pennant` | `rank` | The rank of a result, on a flag that is the same for every rank |
| `Note` | `label`, `open`, `onToggle` | The key of a source, which opens in place to what it holds |
| `Approx`, `NoData` | `says`, which is left out but under the working, and `className` | The mark of what is not whole with the two words "approx data", and a step that holds nothing with the two words "no data". The mark is dress, and the words are read. One part draws each on every page: the lines of a result, its fit and its working, the page of an area, a comparison and the key. Their words are in `content/kit.ts`, and nowhere else. Until 2026-09-27 two parts of one name drew the first, from words in two files |
| `Burro`, `BurroOfEveryPage` | `pose`, which is `sits` or `hops`, `stage`, `peeps`, `ofEveryPage`, `atRest` and `soil` | The rabbit. He says nothing: the page says it, and nothing of him is heard, takes a press or takes the focus. Where he rests he stirs, each movement on a clock of its own. **Nothing stops him but the system**: where it asks for less movement he is still, wherever he is drawn. Until the evening of 2026-09-26 a button stood in the corner of his box that stopped him, and the page kept in memory that he was stopped. `hops` is drawn only while a person waits for a first answer. As he hops soil flicks up from his hole, as he comes up and as he goes down, since 2026-09-27: it is drawn inside the strip of his hop, so it is never out of step with him, and where he is still there is none (`SOIL` in `kit/Burro/look.ts`). `BurroOfEveryPage` is he as what every page stands in draws him, in the name board and over the foot, seen from his back up: where the page draws him itself he is drawn once, and not there (`onThePage.ts`) |
| `Art` | `name`, `alt` | One drawing by its name, at its own size times the pixel of the look, never stretched and never at a fraction. With words it is a picture that says them, and with none it is kept from a screen reader |

**The town of an area**, in `src/components/Town/` and `src/lib/town/` ([ADR 0034](../adr/0034-the-town-of-an-area-is-drawn-by-rule-and-is-no-picture-of-the-place.md)).

| Component | Runs | Props | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Town` | Server or browser | `marks`, where the area sits on each vibe, `meta`, the vibes of the release, `of`, the name of the area for a screen reader, `written`, `onGrass`, `large` | Area, compare, and every result that has room for one |
| `TownKey` | Server | `meta` | The key, on Vibes |
| `Drawing` | Server or browser | `pieces`, `says`, `large`. The drawing alone. Nothing but `Town` and `TownKey` may draw it, so that what a town says of itself goes wherever one is drawn: what it is made of, to a screen reader, and that a part of it is blank, and why, in sight | `Town`, `TownKey` |

**What the look added to a part that was there.** Each has a usual value, which is what is built, and each is one of the lines that [the design of the look](look.md) lists.

| Component | Prop | What it chooses |
|---|---|---|
| `SettingsPanel` | `standing` | That it stands open with no button of its own to open it: what opens and closes it is the page's, which places it in the panel of Deep search and in the fold named "Refine search" |
| `SiteHeader` | `narrow` | On a narrow screen, the four links as words, or as buttons on a line of their own |
| `StatusLine` | `frame`, `room`, `open` | In a box, in no frame of its own under 40rem, or in none at all (`none`). The room of two lines kept, or given up. That a search is open, so that the line says that nothing is ranked yet where nothing is |
| `PromptBox` | `yields`, `help` | That another button of the page matters more than Search just now, so that Search is drawn plain: no page says so since Burro asks nothing. Where what a useful sentence holds stands on a narrow screen: under the field, or over it (`HELP_ON_A_NARROW_SCREEN`) |
| `SearchApp`, `SearchView` | `opens`, `refines`, `gives`, `over` | What becomes of the page as a search opens of a setting that was moved: the setting held where it stood, or the answer brought into sight. Where the fold of the settings stands on a narrow screen: after the first result, or before it. It comes in the page where it is drawn. When what stands over the answer gives way further, and what stands over the list |
| `SearchView`, `SharedSearch` | `bands` | Where every area sits, from the page, handed on to the list of results. The page a shared link opens is built with it as the search page is |
| `Disclosure` | `bring`, `rest` | That what a press opens is brought into sight where it would begin under the foot of what it is seen through: the table of all areas, the fold of the settings, the panel that shares a search and the fold that says a search came from a link. What a bar stands on at rest |
| `ChipRow` | `art` | The chips small on a wide screen, where what stands over the answer must give way |
| `Wait` | `room` | A box as high as a first result with what Burro understood over it, or as high as a first result alone |
| `Strip` | `short`, `ends`, `rests` | The lines in short, on a row. The two ends of a gauge by their pictures alone, or each named under its picture. Whether a band that is not whole says so beside its gauge |
| `Track` | `part`, `small` | A chequered step, where a band rests on part of what its vibe needs. Small steps, on a row |
| `AreaProfile` | `townDrawn`, `townStands` | The town at the pixel of the ground, or of the screen. After the name, or before it |
| `Portrait` | `lists`, `things` | Each list of vibes in a box of its own, or all in one. The drawing of a vibe beside its name, or nowhere |
| `CompareView`, `CompareTable` | `marks`, `towns`, `character` | Where the chosen areas sit on each vibe, from the page, and the towns that are drawn from it |
| `ResultList` | `bands`, `townDrawn`, `lineStands`, `over`, `onANarrowResult`, `inviteStands`, `buttons`, `ends`, `tradeOff`, `others`, `fitSaid` | Where every area sits on every vibe, from the page, so that the town of a result is the town of the page of its area: `marksOn` in `ResultList/town.ts` puts it with the strip. How large a town is. The line of the towns on no result, once of every town of the list, or under each town. What stands over the list. Whether a result with no room draws a town all the same. Where what says that areas can be compared stands. How high the buttons of a narrow result are. Whether the two ends of a gauge are pictured alone or named too. Whether the trade-off stands on a band or in a box. Whether the vibes nobody asked for stand on a result. Whether what a fit is based on is said under the working or on the result. `ResultList/look.ts` holds what each is where the page does not say, and `src/content/towns.ts` the line |

## 8. Design tokens

All tokens are custom properties in `src/styles/tokens.css`. **There is one look.** It has no dark colours: no style sheet asks the system which it prefers, and the browser is told that the page is light and only light, so that it draws its own controls to match. The look is Town Map, and [ADR 0033](../adr/0033-the-website-looks-like-the-map-of-a-gentle-game.md) has what was decided and why. [The design of the look](look.md) says where each thing here is changed.

Until 2026-09-26 this section held two sets of values, a light and a dark, in the faces of the system on white. They are gone, with the switch that was never built.

**The colours of the look.** Seventeen are Town Map's, and three more are greens of the map and of nothing else. No style sheet writes a colour out, and no drawing holds any other. Ratios are worked out by the WCAG formula and rounded down. `tokens.test` works them out again and fails below the figure needed.

| Colour | Value | What it is for | On cream |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--ink` | `#2a2140` | Words, rules, outlines | 13.3 |
| `--page` | `#f7f1de` | Cream: a box, a card, whatever is read on | |
| `--lea` | `#dbe8b1` | The ground where no tile is drawn | |
| `--sand` | `#e9cf94` | A chip in hand, a fold that is closed, land with no fit, and since 2026-09-27 the band of a trade-off on a result. Ink alone is read on it, at 9.9 | |
| `--meadow` | `#a8d85e` | The ground in tiles, the second band of the map | |
| `--verge` | `#63b34a` | The third band of the map | |
| `--hedge` | `#2b7a5a` | What is good, as a journey that is within its limit, and the board over where to go and look on the page of an area. Until 2026-09-27 the board of "Why it fits" on a result, with cream on it at 4.6 | 4.6 |
| `--shallows` | `#86dde2` | Water | |
| `--haven` | `#2f9fd0` | The glint on water | |
| `--cobalt` | `#3453d1` | The one button that matters most in sight, links, the focus ring. Cream is read on it at 5.6 | 5.6 |
| `--amber` | `#f6b426` | What is on or chosen, the band of a notice, a gauge as it fills. Ink is read on it at 8.2. It is never the colour of words | 1.6 |
| `--poppy` | `#db533b` | The edge of a fault, the edge of a button that takes a thing away, and the mark before a journey that is over its limit, in the table of journeys. It is never the colour of words. **It is the mark of no trade-off since 2026-09-27**, and of nothing on a result as it is first shown: red says danger, and what an area gives up is none | 3.4 |
| `--heath`, `--slate` | `#b87bc6`, `#6d7fa6` | In drawings alone | |
| `--fur`, `--earth` | `#9b8a7f`, `#a9683d` | The rabbit, and his burrow, and nothing else. A test holds it | |
| `--shade` | `#65566a` | The hard shadow of a box, and words that are quieter | 6.0 |
| `--dew`, `--fern`, `--ivy` | `#e6f1c5`, `#3c9952`, `#317e5d` | The first, the fourth and the fifth band of the map | |

**The names a part asks for.** A style sheet names a colour by a token, and never by its value. A part that was there before the look names what a colour is for, as `var(--text)` and `var(--chosen)`. The kit, and much of what the look dressed, names a colour of the look itself: `var(--ink)` stands in 45 sheets, `var(--page)` in 37 and `var(--sand)` in 27. So the value of a colour is changed in the tokens and no sheet is touched, and what a colour is used for is changed part by part.

| Token | Is | Used for | Against | Ratio | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `--bg`, `--surface` | `--page` | The ground of whatever is read. Both are cream: nothing is read on the grass, and on sand a quiet word is 4.4, a link 4.1 and the green of what is good 3.4 | | | |
| `--text` | `--ink` | Text | `--bg` | 13.3 | 4.5 |
| `--muted` | `--shade` | Labels, dates | `--bg` | 6.0 | 4.5 |
| `--accent` | `--cobalt` | Links, the main button | `--bg` | 5.6 | 4.5 |
| `--on-accent` | `--page` | Words on the main button | `--accent` | 5.6 | 4.5 |
| `--border` | `--ink` | Edges of fields, buttons and boxes | `--bg`, `--meadow`, `--lea`, `--chosen` | 13.3, 9.0, 11.6, 8.2 | 3 |
| `--focus` | `--cobalt` | The focus ring | `--bg` | 5.6 | 3 |
| `--chosen`, `--on-chosen` | `--amber`, `--ink` | What is on or chosen, and the words on it | Each other | 8.2 | 4.5 |
| `--notice-bg`, `--notice-text`, `--notice-edge`, `--notice-mark` | `--page`, `--ink`, `--ink`, `--amber` | A notice: cream within a rule of ink, with a band of amber at its start. The banner that says the data is made up is one | Text on ground. Edge on band | 13.3. 8.2 | 4.5. 3 |
| `--info-bg`, `--info-text` | `--page`, `--ink` | The banner of a preview | Each other | 13.3 | 4.5 |
| `--good` | `--hedge` | What is good | `--bg` | 4.6 | 4.5 |
| `--tradeoff`, `--tradeoff-mark` | `--ink`, `--poppy` | What is said of a journey that is over its limit, in the table of journeys, and the mark before it. Until 2026-09-27 the words of a trade-off and its mark: a trade-off is ink on sand since, and the two tokens kept their names | `--bg` | 13.3, 3.4 | 4.5, 3 |
| `--error`, `--error-edge` | `--ink`, `--poppy` | The words of a failure, and its edge | `--bg` | 13.3, 3.4 | 4.5, 3 |
| `--map-water`, `--map-glint` | `--shallows`, `--haven` | Water, and the glints on it | `--map-line` | 9.6, 5.0 | 3 |
| `--map-land` | `--sand` | An area with no fit | `--map-line` | 9.9 | 3 |
| `--map-1` to `--map-5` | `--dew`, `--meadow`, `--verge`, `--fern`, `--ivy` | The bands of a fit, low to high | `--map-line` | 12.7, 9.0, 5.8, 4.2, 3.0 | 3 |
| `--map-line` | `--ink` | Outlines, patterns, the edge of a pin and its number | `--bg` | 13.3 | 4.5 |

**Nothing is drawn see-through.** Every ratio above is of two colours at full strength. A list dimmed to 70% put muted text on white at 3.5 to 1 and an edge at 2.5. So no style sets an opacity, a filter or a colour with a fourth part, and `tokens.test` fails if one does. A state is said in words.

One band differs from the next by 1.37 to 1.57 to 1, and `tokens.test` fails under 1.35. That is not enough to read a score from, and nothing asks anyone to: section 6 gives the figure in words wherever the band is shown. Land with no fit is 1.2 from the first band, and is told from it by having no grain.

| Scale | Values |
|---|---|
| The faces | Two, each a file of the website's own under `public/fonts/`, served from its own origin under the SIL Open Font License, with its licence beside it. `--font-name` is Jersey 10, which is drawn in pixels: the name of the website, the name of an area, a heading and a short label. `--font-say` is Rubik: every sentence and every figure. No sheet outside `src/styles` names a face. Each file is asked for only where a letter of it is drawn, and until it arrives the words stand in a face of the system, so no page waits for a face. One file is asked for with every page, ahead of the style sheets (`app/layout.tsx`): that of Jersey 10 which holds the letters of English, which every page draws its name board in. The face of the system that stands in for it is far wider, and a page that asked for the file once its style sheets had come jumped as it arrived: on a phone by 0.16, where 0.1 is held to be the most a page should move, and by 0.002 since it is asked for ahead. A page that is built holds the asking in its head, and one that is drawn when it is asked for, as a comparison is, is answered with it in the header `Link`. `check:pages` holds every built page to it |
| Sizes of the face of names | 20, 30, 40 and 60 px, which are whole tens of its grid: `--name-1` to `--name-4`. A name or a large heading is 30 px or more, and a short label 20. It is never heavy and never slanted: it has one weight, and a browser is told to make up none |
| Sizes of the face of a sentence | 15, 16, 17 and 20 px: `--size-small`, `--size-body`, `--size-lead`, `--size-h3`. Nothing that is read is under 15. Weights 400 to 700, by a figure. A slanted word is plain, since the slanted file holds one weight. Figures in a column ask for figures of one width |
| Headings | The first and the second are in the face of names: 40 and 30 px, and 60 and 40 from 60rem. The third and the fourth are in the face of a sentence, heavy, at 20 and 17 |
| Lines | Line height 1.5 for text and 1.1 for headings. Lines of 58 characters at most |
| What the faces lack | Rubik has no arrow, no tick and no figure set low. None is written: a drawing stands for an arrow or a tick, and the API's words hold none. `faces.test` reads both files, and fails on a character of the recorded answers or of the site copy that the face of a sentence lacks |
| Space | 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64 px. No corner is rounded |
| The pixel | `--px` is how many pixels of the screen one pixel of a drawing takes: 2, and 3 from 60rem. A drawing is never shown at a fraction. `--px-stage` is 5, for the rabbit where he hops. `--px-small` is 2 on every screen, for a control. `--px-ground` is 2, for the meadow |
| The box | `--frame-box` and `--frame-box-on` are the pictures of its rule, cut in nine. `--box-shadow` is its hard shadow, two pixels of a drawing down and to the right, in `--shade`. Whatever throws a hard shadow names that token, so that one line takes every shadow away |
| The ground | `--ground` is the picture of the meadow, laid behind every page, which goes with the page as it is scrolled. Under it the body is `--lea` |
| The page | `--page-width` is 75rem. `--columns` is the two columns of the search page from 60rem: the answer, and the map beside it, each as a share of the page, which is seven to six. Until 2026-09-26 it was three columns from 80rem, with the settings in the first |
| Targets | `--target` 44 px for the main controls. `--target-min` 24 px for the small ones, such as "Source", with 8 px between two of them. What is pressed in a chip of a narrow row is 36 |
| Breakpoints | 40rem and 60rem. A part that stands in a column is laid out by the width of its own box, and not of the screen. No style sheet asks for 80rem since no column of settings stands open |
| Focus | A 3 px ring in `--focus` between two 2 px bands of `--bg`, on `:focus-visible`. The ring has the same contrast on any ground, the grass and the map included. It is never removed |
| Motion | The length of every movement is nought unless the system says that movement is welcome: 120 and 200 ms for the map and a scroll, `--motion-hop` 1,250 ms for one round of the rabbit's hop, which was 1,000 until 2026-09-27, `--motion-drop` 400 ms for a chip that drops into its place in four steps, `--motion-press` 80 ms. What moves, moves in held frames and is never eased. Nothing but the rabbit moves without end. He moves wherever he is drawn, since 2026-09-26: each of his movements has a length and a wait of its own, and the lengths share no factor, so that he is not seen for a loop. Nothing shimmers. With reduced motion the map jumps, scrolling is instant, the rabbit is still wherever he is drawn and throws no soil, a first search waits for nothing, no chip drops, and the words of a button stay where they are under a press. Nothing else stills the rabbit: the button that stopped him went on the evening of 2026-09-26. How long a first search waits is so many rounds of `--motion-hop`, so the one line that stills him takes the wait away with his hop |
| Forced colours | The ground is not drawn. The picture of a box is taken away, and its plain edge is drawn in a colour of the system: a picture is taken away with `border-image-source: none`, since `border-image: none` would set the width of the picture back and let it cover the edge. The edge that a box keeps clear, for its rule and its shadow, is the colour of the ground there: a browser that forces colours draws a clear edge in the colour of words, and every box had a band of it at its right and its foot. Outlines, patterns and the focus ring use system colours. The bands fall away and the figures remain. Drawings stay: each is dress, and what it stands for is said in words |

## 9. Accessibility plan

**The website makes no claim of a standard of accessibility**, since the evening of 2026-09-26 ([ADR 0042](../adr/0042-the-website-makes-no-claim-of-a-standard-of-accessibility.md)). Until then a page of the website said that it aimed at the Web Content Accessibility Guidelines, version 2.2, at level AA, and this section was the plan to that. The page is gone, and the plan stays: it is what the website is built to, for whoever changes it, and every test it names stands. The first column names the point of the guidelines that a row was written to, so that a row can be found. It claims nothing to a visitor, and one row is not met: a person cannot pause, stop or hide the rabbit. What is known to fall short, and what nobody has tested, is said at the foot of this section, where the statement said it.

| WCAG 2.2 | How it is met | How it is tested |
|---|---|---|
| 1.1.1 Non-text content | The map has `AreaTable`. `LocatorMap` has a title that names the area, and the list of neighbouring areas beside it says what it shows. Marks and pips are hidden from a reader and their meaning is in the text beside them. **What is taken from sight on a result is still said to a screen reader.** The gauge of a line of a result is one picture, and its name says the band in words, the two ends the bands are counted between, and whether the thing was asked for: since 2026-09-27 no word of it is written beside the steps, so the name of the picture is where whoever hears the page is told where the area sits. A gauge that holds no peg says that it has five empty steps and no peg. The two words that say a figure is not whole, and the two that say it is not known, are read, and the mark before each is kept from a reader. On the page of an area and in a comparison the band is written in words beside the steps, and the steps are kept from a reader, who would hear it twice. A drawing of the look is dress: the thing of a chip and of a line, the pennant, the key of a source, the picture at each end of a gauge, what is drawn for a trade-off and Burro are each kept from a reader, and what each stands for is said in words. The town of an area is a picture that says what it is, and each of its four parts band by band | `test_everything_the_map_shows_is_in_the_table`: each area's rank, fit and reason appears in the table. `test_the_drawing_of_him_says_nothing_to_a_screen_reader_whatever_he_does`. `test_it_says_to_a_screen_reader_what_it_is_made_of_band_by_band_in_the_words_of_the_service`. Of a result: `test_whoever_hears_the_page_is_still_told_in_words_where_the_area_sits`, `test_a_scale_says_both_its_ends_and_the_one_asked_for_in_the_name_of_its_picture`, `test_the_picture_of_a_mark_says_what_it_shows_and_nothing_is_kept_for_a_screen_reader_alone`, `test_the_picture_of_an_end_is_dress_of_one_size_and_neither_end_is_marked_as_the_better` |
| 1.3.1 Info and relationships | One `h1` a page, and no heading that skips a level. Results are an ordered list. Tables have header cells and say what they are tables of. Groups of controls are fieldsets with legends | axe on every component and on every state of section 3. `check:pages`, on every page as it was built |
| 2.4.2 Page titled, 3.1.1 Language of page | Every page has a title of its own and says it is in British English | `check:pages`. The one page that fails is the framework's own (section 12) |
| 1.4.1 Use of colour | The table in section 6. "Assumed", "firm limit", "within" and "over" are words. Confidence is a word. What is on is amber, and says that it is pressed, chosen or open: a tab that is chosen says so, and so does a group of the settings that is open, whose arrow has turned as well. A chip that nobody said says "assumed", and one that counts for nothing has its thing in outline and the words "does not count". No edge is dashed: a dashed edge said "assumed" a second time until 2026-09-26, and was not understood. A fault has an edge of poppy, and says what went wrong in ink. What an area gives up says so in a word, "Trade-off", beside a drawing, on a band of its own: nothing of it is poppy, and nothing on a result is red to say danger. That a figure is not whole is said in two words beside its mark, and a step is chequered where a band rests on part of what its vibe needs: it is never told by colour | `test_rank_and_status_are_in_words_wherever_they_are_in_colour`, `test_a_trade_off_stands_out_by_where_it_stands_and_how_it_is_drawn_and_nothing_of_it_is_red` |
| 1.4.3, 1.4.11 Contrast | The tokens of section 8, never dimmed. Every sentence, figure and link is read on cream, and ink alone on sand. Nothing is read on the grass, on water or on the land of the map: a name on the map stands on a plate of cream | `tokens.test`, which also fails on any style that draws see-through. `test_what_is_read_is_read_on_cream_and_never_on_the_grass`. On the page of an area and on the pages that explain, a test walks every piece of text and fails on one that stands in no box |
| 1.4.4, 1.4.10, 1.4.12 Resize, reflow, text spacing | Sizes in rem, and nothing that is read under 15 px. One column at 320 px. No fixed heights on text: a box, a button and a chip grow with their words. The journeys, the breakdown, the comparison and the table of every area are stacked on a narrow screen | That each stacked table says its roles and names each cell: `test_a_journey_can_be_stacked_on_a_narrow_screen_and_is_still_a_table`. The layout itself by hand, in a browser |
| 1.4.13 Content on hover or focus | Nothing appears on hover alone. A disclosure stays until closed, and Escape closes it | Keyboard tests |
| 2.1.1, 2.1.2 Keyboard, no trap | Every control is a native element. The combobox follows the ARIA pattern, and so do the two tabs a search begins at: the arrow keys move between them and go round at either end, Home and End go to the first and the last, the tab a key leads to is chosen as it takes the focus, and Tab goes from the chosen tab into its panel. A group of the settings is a button that says whether it is open, and the whole of its bar takes the press. On the map, arrows pan, plus and minus zoom, pins are buttons in rank order, and Tab leaves. Sharing, the working of a result, the settings and the table open in place and trap nothing | `test_a_search_can_be_made_and_refined_by_keyboard_alone`, `test_a_search_can_be_shared_by_keyboard_alone`, `test_areas_can_be_chosen_and_the_comparison_reached_by_keyboard_alone`. Of the tabs: `test_the_arrow_keys_move_between_the_tabs_and_go_round_at_either_end`, `test_home_and_end_go_to_the_first_and_the_last`, `test_tab_goes_from_the_chosen_tab_into_its_panel_and_never_to_the_other_tab` |
| 2.4.1 Bypass blocks | "Skip to results", "Skip to the map" and "Skip the map" | Keyboard tests. `test_there_is_a_link_that_skips_to_the_map`, `test_there_is_a_link_that_skips_the_map`, `test_before_a_search_no_link_leads_to_results_that_are_not_there` |
| 2.4.3, 2.4.7, 2.4.11 Focus order, visible, not obscured | DOM order is reading order, but for the ways on of a row, which are drawn before its strip and come after it: the strip of a row holds nothing a keyboard stops at. Since 2026-09-27 nor do the lines of a card: no line opens, and a result holds four stops, which are its name, the way to compare, its working and what is like it, where a card held eight to ten. The order of the page is the order it is drawn in on every screen: on a narrow screen a keyboard reaches the first result, and then what opens the settings, which is one stop while the fold is closed. Before a search, where the page is one column, it reaches the map first, with a link that skips it. The ring of section 8. The banner scrolls away, and `scroll-padding` clears what sticks: the tray of areas to compare, at the foot of the screen. The button of Deep search is held there too, before a search, and what takes the focus among the space requirements is brought clear of its bar as well, by as much as the bar is high: the page measures the bar, which grows with what Burro says in it. The focus is never left on nothing: what is removed gives it to what is beside it, what goes when a search opens gives it to what Burro understood, and a button that may be pressed until it does nothing is said to be off and is not switched off | Keyboard tests, and by hand. `test_removing_a_chip_moves_the_focus_to_the_chip_beside_it_and_never_leaves_it_on_nothing`, `test_a_pin_that_is_pressed_hands_the_focus_to_the_card_it_opened_and_says_that_it_is_open`, `test_the_button_is_never_switched_off_so_that_it_never_drops_the_focus`. Of the whole page: `test/access/ways.test.tsx`, `settings.test.tsx` and `rabbit.test.tsx` |
| 2.5.7 Dragging | `WeightSlider` has buttons and a number field. A scale has a button at each end. The map has buttons and keys | `test_every_slider_can_be_set_without_dragging` |
| 2.5.8 Target size | `--target` and `--target-min`. A link that takes either is drawn as a box, so that the size holds: a link in a line of text has no height to set. A button of the look is 44 px high or more, and its shadow is room of its own. On a narrow row what is pressed in a chip is 36 px, and on a narrow result a button is 34, which is under the 44 of a main control and over the 24 that is asked: each gave way to the answer. A box that is ticked is 22 by 22, inside a label 44 high that takes the press | `test_every_control_takes_a_target_size`: each interactive component carries one of the two classes. `test_a_link_that_stands_alone_takes_a_target_size`, on every page. Real sizes by hand |
| 3.2.2 On input | A control changes results, never the page or the focus | `test_moving_a_control_does_not_move_focus` |
| 3.3.1, 3.3.2 Errors and labels | Every field has a visible label. An error is text under its field, tied to it with `aria-describedby` | axe, and `test_an_edit_the_api_refuses_puts_the_control_back_and_says_why_beside_it` |
| 3.3.7 Redundant entry | The store carries the search to compare and back | `test_compare_uses_the_search_that_is_open` |
| 4.1.2 Name, role, value | Native elements first. ARIA only for the combobox, the disclosure and the two tabs, which say `tablist`, `tab` and `tabpanel`, which is chosen, and which panel each names, for the picture of a mark of the strip, and for the roles of the comparison table, which are said again because its rows are stacked on a narrow screen. What is seen on a short button is the start of its name: "Compare: Alderwick" | axe. `test_the_button_that_fills_the_comparison_says_what_it_does_in_words_that_are_in_its_name` |
| 4.1.3 Status messages | `StatusLine` is a polite live region. **An answer that the line says in the words of the last is said again**: what it says of a ranking is drawn anew with every answer, where until 2026-09-27 a budget set and cleared three times changed the list six times and the line once. That a search is read is still said once, and that a search was stopped is said in the same line. `ErrorBlock` is an alert. `NoticeBlock` is a status. What showing an area on the map did is a status too, under the three actions of a working and over the table of all areas: each is on the page from the first and holds nothing until the press, so that what comes to stand in it is heard | `test_each_state_is_announced_once`, `test_an_answer_that_is_said_in_the_words_of_the_last_is_said_again`, `test_show_on_the_map_says_what_it_did_and_the_focus_goes_to_the_box_the_map_opened_for_the_area`, `test_what_a_press_said_takes_no_room_until_it_speaks_and_is_said_once`, `test_stop_is_said_in_the_line_that_is_heard_and_the_next_search_says_what_came_of_it`, `test_show_in_a_row_says_what_it_did_and_gives_the_focus_to_the_box_of_the_area` |
| 2.2.2 Pause, stop, hide | **Not met.** One thing moves of itself: the rabbit, on every page, for as long as the page is open. It is more than five seconds, and it is beside what is read. Until the evening of 2026-09-26 a button stood by him that stopped him, which a keyboard reached and a screen reader named. The founder asked for it to go, and relies on what a person has set in their system: where it asks for less movement he is still, and nothing on a page stills him. Whether a setting of the system is a way to pause, stop or hide, as the guidelines mean it, was read nowhere. While a search is read, "Stop" beside Search ends the search, and his hop with it. Nothing else moves without end, and nothing blinks or shimmers | `test_no_button_stands_by_him_and_nothing_of_him_takes_a_press_or_the_focus`, `test_nothing_of_him_is_heard_and_no_button_stands_by_him`, `test_a_person_who_asked_for_less_movement_is_shown_none`, `test_one_line_draws_him_still_as_he_was`, `test_nothing_of_the_wait_moves_but_the_rabbit_himself` |
| Reduced motion | Section 8. The length of every movement is a token, which is nought unless the system says that movement is welcome. Where less movement is asked for the rabbit does not move at all, wherever he is drawn, no soil is drawn, and a first search waits for nothing, since there is nothing to watch | `test_nothing_moves_when_reduced_motion_is_set`, `test_the_length_of_every_movement_is_a_token_which_is_nought_until_movement_is_welcome`, `test_a_person_who_asked_for_less_movement_is_shown_none`, `test_where_the_length_of_a_hop_is_nought_nothing_moves_and_he_waits` |
| A press lands where it was aimed | Nothing moves or changes size under a press, the pointer or the focus, but the face inside a button, which steps by no more than its shadow is wide. What a press opens is brought into sight after the press has landed | `test_nothing_moves_or_changes_size_because_the_focus_or_the_pointer_came_or_went`, `test_a_button_that_itself_moves_under_a_press_is_refused`, `src/lib/sight.test.ts` |

axe is `axe-core`, run in jsdom. It cannot judge contrast or layout, which is why the tokens have their own test. **Not tested by a person**: a screen reader, 200% zoom, reflow at 320 px, forced colours, real target sizes on a touch screen, the map under a keyboard, and anything that moves. The rabbit, a chip that drops and a button under a press were each looked at frame by frame, in still pictures: nobody has watched one move. Nobody has used the two tabs or the accordion of the settings with a screen reader. The look was seen in one browser, Chrome, on one machine, and in no Safari, no Firefox and on no phone. The statement of accessibility said so to a visitor until it went, and this section says it since, under "What nobody has tested".

**Checks to make by hand, in a browser.** Each is of something a browser showed that a test can hold only in part, or not at all. jsdom lays nothing out, so where a test exists it reads the style sheet or the order of the page, and cannot say what is on the screen. Run the website against the service (the README says how), and make each check at 390 by 844 and at 1440 by 900 unless it says otherwise. The figures are what was measured on 2026-09-23, after the changes this section describes, but in checks 1, 14, 19, 23, 35 to 42 and 49 to 53, whose figures are of 2026-09-26. **On 2026-09-26 checks 1, 14, 19, 23, 37 and 49 to 53 were made of the website as it then stood, by software that drives a browser. On 2026-09-27 checks 1, 14, 16, 17, 19, 23, 37, 49 and 52 to 56 were made so again, of the website as it stands since the founder walked it a third time**: what each showed is said in its row, and sections 4.1 and 4.2 have the figures of checks 14 and 19. Nobody made them by hand, and nobody watched anything move. No other check was made again once the founder had walked the website. Checks 43 to 48 are of what was rebuilt after the first walk, and checks 49 to 53 of what was rebuilt after the second.

| # | Do | Expect | What a test holds of it |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | On a phone, open "Try an example", press the first example and then Search.
